01往年考点重点

Vascular smooth muscle & arterial tone

（2020 + 2022 都出现）

Q1 – Mechanisms of contraction and relaxation

Question

问What mechanisms make the vascular smooth muscle cell contract and relax?
答

🔹考试作答推荐（英文箭头版）

1
Contraction of vascular smooth muscle

Contraction of vascular smooth muscle occurs through the following Ca²⁺-dependent pathway:

Intracellular Ca²⁺ ↑ (via L-type voltage-gated Ca²⁺ channels and/or SR release)

→ Ca²⁺–calmodulin complex

→ activation of MLCK (myosin light-chain kinase)

→ phosphorylation of myosin light chain (MLC, Ser19)

→ actin–myosin cross-bridge cycling ↑

→ vascular smooth muscle contraction (vasoconstriction)

1
Relaxation of vascular smooth muscle

Relaxation occurs when:

Intracellular Ca²⁺ ↓→Ca²⁺–calmodulin↓ →MLCK activity ↓ + MLCP (myosin light-chain phosphatase) dephosphorylates MLC

→ actin–myosin cross-bridge cycling ↓

→ vascular smooth muscle relaxation (vasodilation)

🔹背诵用中文逻辑 + 关键词

收缩：细胞内 Ca²⁺↑（L 型电压依赖 Ca 通道 / SR 释放）→ Ca²⁺–钙调蛋白复合物（Ca²⁺–calmodulin）→ 激活 MLCK（myosin light-chain kinase）→ 磷酸化肌球蛋白轻链 MLC（Ser19）→ 肌动-肌球蛋白交联增加（cross-bridge cycling↑）→ 血管平滑肌收缩（vasoconstriction）

舒张：细胞内 Ca²⁺↓ → Ca²⁺–calmodulin 减少 → MLCK 活性下降＋ MLCP（myosin light-chain phosphatase）去磷酸化 MLC→ 交联减少（cross-bridge cycling↓）→ 平滑肌舒张 → 血管扩张（vasodilation）

一句话核心：Ca²⁺–MLCK 促收缩，MLCP 促舒张，两者平衡决定 vascular tone。

Q2 – Arterial diameter, blood flow and blood pressure

Question

问Why is arterial diameter important for determining blood flow and blood pressure?
答

🔹考试作答推荐（英文版）

Arterial diameter is a major determinant of vascular resistance.

According to Poiseuille’s law, resistance is inversely proportional to the fourth power of the radius, so small changes in arterial diameter produce very large changes in resistance.

Vasoconstriction (decrease in diameter) increases peripheral resistance, reduces blood flow and tends to raise arterial blood pressure.

Conversely, vasodilation (increase in diameter) decreases resistance, facilitates blood flow and tends to lower arterial blood pressure.

你也可以简化成箭头写法：

Arterial diameter ↓ (vasoconstriction)

→ resistance ↑ (1 / r⁴)

→ blood flow ↓

→ arterial blood pressure ↑

Arterial diameter ↑ (vasodilation)

→ resistance ↓

→ blood flow ↑

→ arterial blood pressure ↓

🔹背诵用中文逻辑 + 关键词

阻力 ~ 1 / r⁴（Poiseuille 定律，radius⁴ 非常敏感）

动脉直径减小（vasoconstriction）→ 外周阻力↑（peripheral resistance）→ 器官血流量↓（blood flow）→ 动脉血压↑（arterial blood pressure）

动脉直径增大（vasodilation）→ 阻力↓ → 血流↑ → 血压↓

一句话核心：动脉直径通过 1/r⁴ 关系改变外周阻力，从而决定血流和血压。

Q3 – Ex vivo measurement of arterial tone (myography) 动脉张力的离体测量（肌电图）

Question

问Which method can be used to monitor arterial tone ex vivo? What is the principle?
答

🔹考试作答推荐（英文版）

Arterial tone ex vivo can be assessed by myography.

Small arteries are isolated from the animal, cut into short rings and mounted on wires or pins in a myograph chamber containing physiological solution.

The rings are stretched to a defined resting tension, and changes in isometric force are recorded when vasoconstrictor or vasodilator agents are applied.

This allows precise measurement of vascular smooth muscle contraction and relaxation without interference from circulating hormones or the sympathetic nervous system.

也可以用箭头结构写得更像流程：

Take small artery → cut into arterial rings

→ mounted on wires/pins in a myograph chamber (physiological solution)

→ set to a defined resting tension

→ add vasoconstrictor / vasodilator

→ record changes in isometric force

→ evaluation of arterial tone (contraction / relaxation)

🔹背诵用中文逻辑 + 关键词

方法：myography（肌张测定）

操作思路：取小动脉 → 切成短“血管环”（arterial rings）→ 用钢丝/针挂在 myograph 装置里（wires / pins in myograph chamber）→ 浸在生理溶液中，设定固定的初始张力（resting tension）

原理：加入缩血管 / 扩血管药物（vasoconstrictor / vasodilator）→ 记录等长收缩张力的变化（isometric force）→ 定量评估血管平滑肌收缩/舒张 → 反映 arterial tone

优点：体外系统，条件可控，没有循环激素和交感神经干扰。

Excitation–contraction coupling in an adult ventricular cardiomyocyte成人心室心肌细胞的兴奋-收缩耦合

1️⃣ Depolarization & Ca²⁺ entry 去极化 + Ca²⁺ 进入

→ AP along sarcolemma & T-tubules

→ 动作电位沿肌膜和 T 管传播

→ open L-type Ca²⁺ channels → small Ca²⁺ influx

→ 打开 L 型 Ca²⁺ 通道 → 少量 Ca²⁺ 入细胞

2️⃣ Ca²⁺-induced Ca²⁺ release Ca²⁺ 诱导 Ca²⁺ 释放

→ trigger Ca²⁺ activates RyR2 on SR

→ 触发 Ca²⁺ 激活 SR 上的 RyR2

→ massive Ca²⁺ release → [Ca²⁺]ᵢ ↑

→ SR 大量放 Ca²⁺ → 细胞质 [Ca²⁺]ᵢ 升高

3️⃣ Contraction 收缩

→ Ca²⁺ binds troponin C → tropomyosin moves

→ Ca²⁺ 结合 TnC → 原肌球蛋白移开

→ actin–myosin cross-bridge cycling ↑ → sarcomere shortening

→ 横桥循环↑ → 肌节缩短 → 收缩

4️⃣ Relaxation (Ca²⁺ removal) 舒张（清 Ca²⁺）

→ Ca²⁺ leaves troponin C

→ Ca²⁺ 从 TnC 解离

→ SERCA pumps Ca²⁺ back into SR

→ SERCA 把 Ca²⁺ 收回 SR

→ NCX & sarcolemmal Ca²⁺-ATPase extrude Ca²⁺ out of cell

→ NCX 和肌膜 Ca²⁺-ATP 酶把 Ca²⁺ 泵出细胞

→ [Ca²⁺]ᵢ ↓ → cross-bridge cycling ↓ → relaxation

→ 细胞内 [Ca²⁺] 降低 → 横桥循环减少 → 心肌舒张

（Mitochondria take up some Ca²⁺ to match ATP production to contraction.

线粒体也摄取少量 Ca²⁺，用来调节 ATP 生成与收缩匹配。）

Dr Saller

Q1 What are the cardinal features of microvascular endothelial cell activation, and how do endothelial cells contribute to vascular inflammation, increased permeability and thrombus formation? 微血管内皮细胞活化的主要特征是什么？内皮细胞如何导致血管炎症、通透性增加和血栓形成？

A1

1️⃣ Vascular inflammation

→Cytokines, shear stress, injury

→ ↑ adhesion molecules (selectins, ICAM-1, VCAM-1)

→ Leukocytes (white blood cells): rolling → sticking → squeezing through the vessel wall

2️⃣ Increased permeability

→ junction disassembly / cytoskeleton contraction

→ gaps between cells↑

→ plasma proteins & leukocytes cross the wall.

3️⃣ Thrombus formation

→ Endothelial surface becomes pro-thrombotic

→ Platelets stick to vWF and get activated

→ Coagulation cascade on tissue factor

→ Thrombin makes a fibrin mesh → stable thrombus

中文提纲

三大特征
炎症、通透性↑、血栓形成。
炎症
黏附分子↑ → 白细胞黏附穿出。
通透
连接松→蛋白/细胞漏出。
血栓
vWF/TF↑ → 血小板+凝血→纤维蛋白网。
问Q2 How does the receptor Tie2 maintain endothelial cells in a quiescent state? 受体Tie2如何维持内皮细胞处于静止状态？
答

A2

Pericytes / mural cells （周细胞 / 壁细胞）

→ secrete Angiopoietin-1 (Ang-1) 分泌血管生成素-1 (Ang-1)

→ Ang-1 binds Tie2 on endothelial cells。 Ang-1 与内皮细胞上的 Tie2 结合

→ Tie2 is activated Tie2 被激活

→ Endothelial cells stay quiet and healthy：内皮细胞保持静止和健康状态：

less inflammation: fewer adhesion molecules炎症减少：黏附分子减少

tight barrier: tight junctions are kept closed屏障紧密：紧密连接保持关闭

less clotting: more anticoagulant, less pro-coagulant factors凝血减少：抗凝因子增多，促凝因子减少

中文提纲

周细胞分泌 Ang-1 激活内皮 Tie2。

维持内皮“安静”：抗炎、维持屏障、抗血栓。

❤️ Dr Piquereau

Q3

问What are the main energy producers and consumers in cardiomyocytes? 心肌细胞中主要的能量产生者和消耗者是什么？
答

A3

Mitochondria are the main ATP producers (oxidative phosphorylation).

ATP is mainly consumed by myofibrillar ATPases (contraction) and ion pumps (Na⁺/K⁺-ATPase, Ca²⁺-ATPases) located close to mitochondria.

中文提纲

主要产能：线粒体氧化磷酸化。

主要耗能：肌丝收缩 + 各种离子泵。

Q4

问How is energy transferred within cardiomyocytes and why are specialized energy-transfer systems required? 心肌细胞内的能量是如何传递的？为什么需要专门的能量传递系统？
答

A4

1️⃣ Creatine kinase shuttle (CKS)

Mitochondrial ATP

→ mitochondrial CK: ATP + creatine → ADP + phosphocreatine (PCr)

→ PCr diffuses through cytosol

→ cytosolic CK near myofibrils: PCr + ADP → ATP + creatine

→ local ATP regeneration at ATPases.

2️⃣ Direct adenine nucleotide channeling (DANC)

Ordered ultrastructure → mitochondria packed near myofibrillar ATPases

→ microdomains / channels allow direct ATP/ADP exchange.

Reason: simple ATP diffusion would be too slow for the very high, localized energy demand of the beating heart.

中文提纲

两套系统：CK 接力棒 + DANC 直通车。

确保高需能位点（收缩装置、离子泵）能快速、高效拿到 ATP。

⚙️ Dr Pereira

Q5

Describe the main Ca²⁺-dependent pathways involved in excitation–contraction coupling in cardiomyocytes and how they are altered in heart failure.

A5

1️⃣ Calcineurin–NFAT pathway

Ca²⁺ ↑ → Ca²⁺–calmodulin → activates calcineurin (phosphatase).

Ca²⁺ 升高 → 形成 Ca²⁺–钙调蛋白复合物 → 激活去磷酸酶 calcineurin。

Calcineurin dephosphorylates NFAT → NFAT enters nucleus → hypertrophic gene transcription.

Calcineurin去磷酸化 NFAT → NFAT 入核 → 启动心肌肥厚相关基因转录。

2️⃣ CaMKII–HDAC–MEF2 pathway

Gq-coupled GPCR agonists (e.g. endothelin) → PLC → IP₃ → nuclear Ca²⁺ ↑.

Gq 偶联受体激动剂（如内皮素）→ 激活 PLC → 生成 IP₃ → 核周 Ca²⁺ 升高。

↑ nuclear Ca²⁺ → activates CaMKII → phosphorylates class II HDAC bound to MEF2.

核内 Ca²⁺ 升高 → 激活 CaMKII → 磷酸化与 MEF2 结合的 II 类 HDAC。

HDAC is exported from nucleus → free MEF2 activates hypertrophic genes.

HDAC 被排出细胞核 → 游离 MEF2 激活肥厚基因表达。

3️⃣ Changes in heart failure 心衰时的改变

In heart failure
CaMKII and calcineurin activities are increased.
心力衰竭时
CaMKII 和 calcineurin 活性升高。

SERCA ↓, NCX ↑ → impaired SR Ca²⁺ reuptake and abnormal Ca²⁺ transients.

SERCA 表达/功能下降，NCX 增加 → SR 回收 Ca²⁺ 受损，Ca²⁺ 瞬变异常。

Result
systolic and diastolic dysfunction, higher risk of arrhythmias.

结果：收缩和舒张功能障碍，心律失常风险升高。

中文提纲

两条 Ca²⁺-依赖肥厚通路
Calcineurin–NFAT、CaMKII–HDAC–MEF2。
心衰
CaMKII/Calcineurin 过度激活，SERCA↓、NCX↑ → Ca²⁺ 处理失衡 → 收缩舒张受损 + 心律失常。

🫀 Dr Vecksler

Q6

Describe the sequence of intracellular disturbances occurring in cardiomyocytes during acute myocardial ischemia and reperfusion, and explain the reversible and irreversible phases. 描述急性心肌缺血及再灌注时心肌细胞内变化，可逆/不可逆阶段。

A6

Early ischemia – reversible 早期缺血（可逆）

→ Coronary flow ↓ → O₂ lack

→ 冠脉灌注↓ → 供氧不足

→ Oxidative phosphorylation stops → ATP ↓

→ 氧化磷酸化停 → ATP↓

→ Switch to anaerobic glycolysis → lactate ↑ → acidosis

→ 改走无氧糖酵解 → 乳酸↑ → 酸中毒

→ Myofilament Ca²⁺ sensitivity ↓ → contractility ↓

→ 肌丝对 Ca²⁺ 敏感性↓ → 收缩力↓

→ Na⁺/K⁺-ATPase failure → K⁺ efflux → extracellular K⁺ ↑ → arrhythmias

→ Na⁺/K⁺ 泵失败 → K⁺ 外流 → 细胞外 K⁺↑ → 心律失常

Reperfusion 再灌注

→ Re-oxygenation + many electrons → massive ROS

→ 氧气突然恢复 + 电子多 → ROS 暴增 → 再灌注损伤

Prolonged ischemia – irreversible 持续缺血（不可逆）

→ Ca²⁺ overload (pump failure)

→ Ca²⁺ 过载（各类离子泵失效）

→ Proteases / lipases / endonucleases activation

→ 各种蛋白酶、脂肪酶、核酸酶被激活

→ Membrane & mitochondria damage → cell swelling, necrosis

→ 膜和线粒体破坏 → 细胞肿胀、坏死

中文一句话提纲

缺血
ATP↓ → 无氧糖酵解/酸中毒 → 收缩↓ → Na/K 泵停 → K⁺ 外流 → 心律失常；
再灌注
氧恢复 → ROS↑；持续缺血：Ca²⁺ 过载 + 各类酶激活 → 膜坏死。中文提纲
可逆
缺血→ATP↓→无氧糖酵解、乳酸、酸中毒→收缩下降；Na/K 泵停→K⁺ 外流→心律失常。
再灌注
氧恢复→ROS 暴增。
不可逆
Ca²⁺ 过载→各类降解酶激活→膜/线粒体破坏→坏死。

🫁 Dr Chemla

Q7

问Briefly describe the mechanisms responsible for maintaining low pressure in the right heart–pulmonary artery system. 右心–肺动脉系统如何维持低压？
答

A7

→ Thin, compliant right atrium/ventricle → low filling pressures

→ 右心房/右心室薄、顺应性好 → 充盈压低

→ Few VSM cells + low sympathetic tone in pulmonary arteries → low resistance

→ 肺动脉平滑肌少、交感张力低 → 肺血管阻力低

→ High compliance of pulmonary arteries → large volume change, small pressure rise

→ 肺动脉弹性好 → 容量变化大，压力变化小

→ Low LV end-diastolic pressure → no backward high pressure to lungs

→ 左心室舒末压低 → 高压不反向传到肺循环

→ Exercise: recruitment/distension of extra pulmonary vessels → flow ↑, pressure still low

→ 运动时通过招募/扩张更多毛细血管来增流，而不是升压

提纲

右心薄顺应高；肺血管肌少阻力低；肺动脉弹性好；LVEDP 低；运动时靠“招募毛细血管”而不是涨压。

💊 Dr Morel

Q8

Describe the clinical and morphological characteristics, risk factors, proposed causes and suggested therapies of anthracycline-induced cardiomyopathy.

A8

Characteristics

Dilated LV, systolic dysfunction, ↓ LVEF, fibrosis, HF symptoms, arrhythmias; usually no hypertrophy.

Risk factors

High cumulative dose / previous exposure

old age or very young

pre-existing heart disease, diabetes, obesity

other cardiotoxic drugs or chest irradiation

genetic susceptibility.

Causes/mechanisms

Anthracyclines inhibit topoisomerase IIβ in cardiomyocytes

redox cycling → ROS overproduction

→ mitochondrial and DNA damage, energetic failure

→ cardiomyocyte death → cardiomyopathy.

Suggested therapies

Early ACE inhibitors + β-blockers

± dose limitation, liposomal forms, dexrazoxane.

中文提纲

典型为扩张型心肌病样改变
左室扩大、LVEF↓、纤维化、心衰/心律失常。
危险因素
累积剂量大、基础心病/代谢病、合并放疗/其他心毒药、年龄、遗传。
机制
Topo IIβ 抑制 + ROS 过多 → 线粒体/DNA 损伤 → 肌细胞死亡。
治疗
早期 ACEI + β 阻滞剂，控制剂量等。

Q9

Describe the main features of the EPAC signalosome and indicate some physiological and pathophysiological roles.

A9

β-adrenergic stimulation

→ Gs → adenylate cyclase → cAMP ↑

→ activation of EPAC (Exchange Protein directly Activated by cAMP)

→ Rap2B → PLC → IP₃ ↑

→ IP₃R-mediated SR Ca²⁺ release

→ activation of Ras/Rac, calcineurin, CaMKII

→ NFAT dephosphorylation + HDAC phosphorylation / MEF2 activation

→ hypertrophic gene expression.

Physiological roles

• Controls osmotic water permeability in kidney collecting duct

• Inhibits lung fibroblast proliferation and collagen synthesis

• Increases insulin secretion in β-cells.

Pathophysiological roles

• Promotes cardiomyocyte death during ischemia (Ca²⁺ overload)

• Involved in autophagy (↑ Beclin-1, LC3-II).

中文提纲

EPAC
cAMP 直接激活 → Rap2B→PLC→IP₃→Ca²⁺ 释放→Calcineurin/CaMKII/MEF2→心肌肥厚基因。
生理
肾集合管水通量、抑制肺成纤维细胞、促胰岛素释放。
病理
缺血时促 Ca²⁺ 过载与细胞死亡、激活自噬。

💉 Dr Manoury – Pharmacology

Q10

Which are the therapeutic targets and mechanisms of action of drugs acting on the sympathetic nervous system? 交感神经药物的靶点和作用机制。

A10

α₁ (Gq, vascular SMC) 血管平滑肌

→ NE → α₁ → PLC → IP₃/DAG → Ca²⁺ ↑ → vasoconstriction → BP ↑

→ 去甲肾上腺素激活 α₁ → Ca²⁺↑ → 血管收缩 → 血压↑

α₂ (Gi, presynaptic) 突触前

→ α₂ → AC ↓ → cAMP ↓ → NE release ↓ → sympathetic tone ↓ → BP ↓

→ 激活 α₂ → cAMP↓ → NE 释放↓ → 交感张力↓ → 血压↓

β₁ (Gs, heart) 心脏

→ β₁ → AC ↑ → cAMP/PKA ↑ → HR ↑, contractility ↑, renin ↑

→ β₁ 激活 → 心率↑、收缩力↑、肾素↑

β₂ (Gs, vessels/bronchi) 血管/支气管

→ β₂ → cAMP/PKA ↑ → smooth muscle relaxation → vasodilation / bronchodilation

→ β₂ 激活 → 平滑肌舒张 → 血管/支气管扩张

提纲

α₁
收缩血管；α₂：抑制 NE；β₁：心脏兴奋 + 肾素；β₂：血管支气管舒张。

Q11

问Which are the therapeutic targets and mechanisms of action of drugs acting on the renin–angiotensin–aldosterone system (RAAS)?
答

A11

RAAS axis RAAS 轴

→ Renin → Ang I → ACE → Ang II

→ Ang II → AT₁ (Gq) → vasoconstriction + Na⁺/water retention + SNS ↑ + remodelling

→ Ang II–AT₁：血管收缩 + Na/水潴留 + 交感↑ + 心血管重构

Drugs 药物

→ Renin inhibitors：↓ Ang I/II

→ 肾素抑制剂：减少 Ang I/II

→ ACE inhibitors：↓ Ang II, ↑ bradykinin

→ ACEI：Ang II↓，缓激肽↑

→ ARBs (AT₁ blockers)：block Ang II effects

→ ARB：阻断 Ang II-AT₁ 效应

→ MR antagonists：block aldosterone

→ 醛固酮受体拮抗剂：阻断 Na 水潴留和纤维化

效果

→ ↓ BP, ↓ remodelling, renal & cardiac protection

→ 降压 + 抗重构 + 保护心肾

Q12

问Which are the therapeutic targets and mechanisms of action of drugs acting on L-type Ca²⁺ channels in heart and vessels?
答

A12

Target 靶点

→ L-type voltage-dependent Ca²⁺ channels in heart & vascular SMCs

→ 心肌与血管平滑肌的 L 型 Ca²⁺通道

DHP CCBs (nifedipine 等)

→ Block vascular L-type Ca²⁺ channels → Ca²⁺ entry ↓

→ 阻断血管 L 型通道 → Ca²⁺ 内流↓

→ Vasodilation → peripheral resistance ↓ → BP ↓, coronary flow ↑

→ 血管舒张 → 外周阻力↓ → 血压↓，冠脉血流↑

Non-DHP (verapamil, diltiazem)

→ Also block nodal/conducting tissue Ca²⁺ entry

→ 还阻断结间传导组织 Ca²⁺ 内流

→ Negative chronotropic/dromotropic effects

→ 降心率、减慢传导

提纲

阻断 L 型 Ca 通道 → 血管舒张降压；verapamil/diltiazem 还降心率、减传导。

Q13

问How can drugs increase oxygen supply to the heart, diminish oxygen demand and inhibit cardiac remodelling?
答

A13

Increase O₂ supply

↓ HR (β-blockers, some CCBs)

→ diastolic time ↑ → coronary perfusion ↑.

Diminish O₂ demand / ↓ BP

↓ HR & contractility (β-blockers, some CCBs)

↓ afterload via vasodilators / ACEI / ARB / CCB

→ myocardial O₂ consumption ↓.

Inhibit remodelling

ACEI + ARB + β-blockers

→ block Ang II and chronic sympathetic stimulation

→ ↓ hypertrophy and fibrosis.

中文提纲

增供氧：降心率 → 舒张期延长 → 冠脉灌注↑。

减需氧：HR↓、收缩力↓、外周阻力↓。

抗重构：ACEI/ARB/β 阻滞剂。

💉 Dr Manoury – Vascular smooth muscle

Q14

问What mechanisms make the vascular smooth muscle cell contract and relax?
答

A14

Contraction 收缩

→ Intracellular Ca²⁺ ↑ (L-type channels / SR release)

→ 细胞内 Ca²⁺↑（L 型通道 + SR 释放）

→ Ca²⁺–calmodulin complex

→ 形成 Ca²⁺–钙调蛋白复合物

→ Activation of MLCK

→ 激活 MLCK

→ MLC phosphorylation (Ser19)

→ 轻链 MLC 磷酸化

→ Actin–myosin cross-bridge cycling ↑ → vasoconstriction

→ 横桥循环↑ → 血管收缩

Relaxation 舒张

→ Intracellular Ca²⁺ ↓ + MLCP activation

→ 细胞内 Ca²⁺↓ + MLCP 活化

→ MLC dephosphorylation

→ MLC 去磷酸化

→ Cross-bridge cycling ↓ → vasodilation

→ 横桥循环↓ → 血管舒张

提纲

收缩：Ca²⁺–CaM → MLCK↑ → MLC-P↑ → 交联↑；

舒张：Ca²⁺↓ + MLCP → MLC 去磷酸 → 交联↓。

Q15

问Why is arterial diameter important for determining blood flow and blood pressure?
答

A15

Poiseuille’s law 泊肃叶定律

→ R ∝ 1 / r⁴

→ 阻力 R 与半径 r 成 1/r⁴ 关系

When diameter (radius) decreases 直径减小

→ r ↓ → R ↑↑↑ → blood flow ↓ → arterial BP ↑

→ 半径↓ → 阻力剧增 → 血流量↓ → 动脉压↑

When diameter increases 直径增大

→ r ↑ → R ↓ → blood flow ↑ → BP ↓

→ 半径↑ → 阻力↓ → 血流↑ → 血压↓

Q16

问Which method can be used to monitor arterial tone ex vivo, and what is its principle?
答

A16

Method: wire myography.

Isolate small artery → cut into rings

→ mount on wires/pins in a myograph chamber with physiological solution

→ set a defined resting tension

→ apply vasoconstrictor / vasodilator agents

→ record changes in isometric force

→ quantify contraction/relaxation (arterial tone) without hormonal or neural influence.

中文提纲

方法：myography（小动脉环挂在钢丝上）。

固定初始张力 → 加缩/舒血管药 → 测等长张力变化 → 评价血管张力。

Lung / Mucosal Immunology

问Q1 什么是黏膜免疫？Type I / Type II mucosal surfaces 有何区别？
What is mucosal immunology? Differences between Type I and Type II mucosal surfaces?
  • A

Mucosal immunology 黏膜免疫

Mucosal immunology = immune responses at mucosal surfaces (gut, respiratory, urogenital) to balance protection and tolerance.

黏膜免疫 = 肠道、呼吸道、泌尿生殖道等黏膜表面的免疫应答，用来在防御和耐受之间保持平衡。

2️⃣ Type I mucosal surfaces I 型黏膜

Type I
simple columnar epithelium + main IgA (secretory IgA); gut, respiratory tract, female upper genital tract.
I 型
单层柱状上皮 + 以 IgA（分泌型 IgA）为主；见于肠道、呼吸道、女性上生殖道。

3️⃣ Type II mucosal surfaces II 型黏膜

Type II
stratified squamous epithelium + main IgG; mouth/eye, female lower genital tract.
II 型
复层鳞状上皮 + 以 IgG 为主；见于口腔/眼表、女性下生殖道。
问Q2 肺黏膜上皮有哪些细胞？各自免疫功能是什么？
Which epithelial cells compose the lung epithelium and what are their immune roles?
  • A（老师 Summary 要求 + 课件补充）

1️⃣ Goblet cells – mucus barrier

→ Produce mucus and trap pathogens/particles.

→ 分泌黏液，粘住病原体和颗粒，形成物理屏障。

2️⃣ Ciliated cells – mucociliary clearance

→ Move mucus upwards to the pharynx for removal.

→ 纤毛摆动，把带病原的黏液往咽部运走并排出。

3️⃣ Club cells – IgA & protection

→ Detox + surfactant-like proteins; express pIgR to transport IgA.

→ 解毒、分泌表面活性样蛋白；表达 pIgR 转运 IgA。

4️⃣ Basal cells – repair

→ Stem/progenitor cells that renew and repair the epithelium.

→ 干/祖细胞，负责上皮更新和修复。

5️⃣ AEC I – gas-exchange barrier

→ Very thin cells for gas exchange and physical barrier.

→ 超薄铺展，完成气体交换并形成物理屏障。

6️⃣ AEC II – surfactant & immune signals

→ Produce surfactant; secrete cytokines and can present antigens.

→ 分泌表面活性物质；分泌细胞因子，并可递呈抗原。

7️⃣ Alveolar macrophages – phagocytosis & inflammation control

→ Phagocytose particles/microbes and regulate local inflammation.

→ 吞噬颗粒和微生物，调控局部炎症，维持稳态。

Gut Immunology

问Q3 肠上皮由哪些细胞组成？各自功能？
Which epithelial and immune cells compose the gut epithelium and what are their roles?
  • A（老师 Summary + 课件细化）

1️⃣ Goblet cells → secrete mucus → physical barrier（杯状细胞：分泌黏液，物理屏障）

2️⃣ Paneth cells → secrete antimicrobial peptides → chemical barrier（潘氏细胞：抗菌肽，化学屏障）

3️⃣ M cells → take up lumen antigens → deliver to APCs（M 细胞：摄取腔内抗原→递给 APC）

4️⃣ Enterocytes → absorb nutrients → sense microbes via PRRs（吸收细胞：营养吸收＋PRR 感知菌群）

5️⃣ IELs (CD8⁺) → patrol epithelium → kill infected cells（上皮内淋巴细胞：巡逻上皮，杀感染细胞）

问Q4 肠黏膜屏障的结构？
What is the structure of the gut mucosal barrier?
  • A（“Inductive site / Effector site” + mucus 双层）

1️⃣ Lumen factors 腔内容物

→ pH, digestive enzymes and bile acids limit pathogenic bacterial growth and colonization.

→ 腔内的 pH、消化酶和胆汁酸可以限制细菌生长和定植。

2️⃣ Mucus layers 黏液双层

→ Goblet cells secrete mucin to form a two-layer mucus barrier.

→ 杯状细胞分泌黏蛋白，形成双层黏液屏障。

→ The inner firm layer is rich in defensins, IgA and AMPs and keeps bacteria away from epithelium.

→ 内层致密黏液富含防御素、IgA 和抗菌肽，阻止细菌靠近上皮。

→ The outer loose layer contains many commensal bacteria.

→ 外层疏松黏液中有大量共生菌。

3️⃣ Epithelial layer 上皮层

→ Columnar epithelial cells joined by tight junctions form a physical barrier.

→ 柱状上皮细胞通过紧密连接连在一起，形成物理屏障。

4️⃣ Lamina propria & immune cells 固有层免疫区

→ In the lamina propria, macrophages, dendritic cells and lymphoid tissue (MALT, Peyer’s patches) provide immune defense.

→ 在固有层内，巨噬细胞、树突状细胞和黏膜淋巴组织（如派尔斑）提供免疫防御。

5️⃣ Gut-vascular barrier 肠血管屏障

→ The vascular endothelium prevents bacteria and toxins from entering the blood.

→ 血管内皮构成肠血管屏障，阻止细菌和毒素进入血液。

背的时候就按：

腔内容物 → 黏液双层 → 上皮 → 固有层免疫细胞 → 肠血管屏障 一路往下背就行。

问Q5 肠道中共生菌 vs 致病菌的免疫反应差异？
How does the immune system respond differently to commensal vs pathogenic bacteria in the gut?
  • A

1️⃣ Goal

→ Immune balance → healthy microbiota and tolerance.

2️⃣ Commensal bacteria

→ Commensals induce tolerogenic DC that produce IL-10/RA → Treg ↑ → inflammation ↓.

3️⃣ Pathogenic bacteria

→ Pathogens induce pro-inflammatory DC that give strong signals → Th1/Th17 ↑ → inflammation ↑.

问Q6 IgA 在肠道菌群中的作用？类型和功能？
What are the types and functions of IgA in controlling gut microbiota?
  • A

1️⃣ Main point 主点

→ IgA = main antibody of gut mucosa, keeps microbiota in balance.

→ IgA 是肠道黏膜的主要抗体，用来维持菌群平衡。

2️⃣ Production 产生方式

→ (1) T-cell-independent IgA：broad, low-affinity coating of many bacteria.

→ ① 无 T 细胞依赖 IgA
亲和力低但包得广，轻度包被很多细菌。

→ (2) T-cell-dependent IgA：high-affinity, more specific for selected bacteria.

→ ② T 细胞依赖 IgA
亲和力高，对特定菌群更有针对性。

3️⃣ Subclasses 亚类

→ IgA1
mainly in proximal gut (small intestine).
→ IgA1
主要在近端肠道，如小肠。
→ IgA2
mainly in distal colon, more resistant to proteases.
→ IgA2
主要在远端结肠，更耐受细菌蛋白酶。

4️⃣ Secretory IgA 分泌型 IgA

→ sIgA = dimeric IgA + J chain, transported by pIgR across epithelium.

→ sIgA = 二聚体 IgA + J 链，经 pIgR 穿过上皮。

→ Secretory component protects sIgA from digestion.

→ 分泌片保护 sIgA 不被消化酶降解。

5️⃣ Exclude bacteria 排除细菌方式

→ Agglutination
IgA cross-links bacteria into clumps that are washed out.
→ 凝集
IgA 把细菌连成团块，被冲走。
→ Enchained growth
IgA links dividing bacteria so they spread less.
→ 连锁生长
IgA 把分裂中的细菌连在一起，不易扩散。

6️⃣ Three key functions 三大功能

→ 1) Prevent colonization of pathogens and harmful commensals.

→ ① 阻止病原体和有害共生菌定植。

→ 2) Support stable colonization of beneficial bacteria and biofilms.

→ ② 促进有益菌稳定定植和生物膜形成。

→ 3) Modulate bacterial gene expression (e.g. flagella, glycan metabolism).

→ ③ 调节细菌基因表达（如鞭毛、糖代谢等）。

问Q7 Bacteroidetes & Helicobacter hepaticus 免疫调节例子 + Th17/Treg 平衡？
Example of immunoregulation between Bacteroidetes and Helicobacter hepaticus and Th17/Treg balance?
  • A

Bacteroidetes → Treg, IL-10 ↑ → brake, anti-inflammatoryBacteroidetes → Treg、IL-10 增加 → 刹车，抗炎

H. hepaticus → Th17, IL-17/IL-23 ↑ → gas, pro-inflammatoryH. hepaticus → Th17、IL-17/IL-23 增加 → 油门，促炎

Balance Th17/Treg → homeostasis.

Treg ↓ + Th17 ↑ + barrier damage → pathogenic colonization → chronic inflammation / IBD.Th17/Treg 平衡 → 稳态；Treg↓ + Th17↑ + 屏障坏 → 有害菌定植 → 慢性炎症 / IBD。

问Q8 IBD 的两种类型及免疫机制？JAK/STAT 靶向治疗的原理？
What are the two types of IBD and the immunological mechanism? Principle of JAK/STAT-targeted therapy?
  • A

Types of IBD 两种类型

Ulcerative colitis (UC) is a chronic inflammation of the colon mucosa with continuous lesions.溃疡性结肠炎（UC）是结肠黏膜的慢性炎症，病变是连续的一大片。

Crohn’s disease 　 can affect any part of the gut and causes patchy, transmural inflammation.克罗恩病（CD）可以累及整个消化道，炎症是跳跃性的斑块，而且可以穿透肠壁全层。
答案 CD

Immunological mechanism 免疫机制

In IBD, the balance between Th17 cells and Treg cells is lost.在 IBD 中，Th17 细胞和 Treg 细胞之间的平衡被打破。

Th17 is too active and Treg is too weak, so inflammation is not well controlled.Th17 过度活跃而 Treg 过弱，所以炎症控制不好。

The mucus barrier is damaged, and harmful bacteria can stay close to the epithelium.黏液屏障受损，有害细菌可以贴近上皮停留。

These changes cause many pro-inflammatory cytokines to increase and lead to chronic intestinal inflammation.这些改变使多种促炎细胞因子升高，从而引起慢性肠道炎症。

JAK/STAT-targeted therapy JAK/STAT 靶向治疗原理

Many cytokines signal through the JAK/STAT pathway to switch on inflammation genes.很多细胞因子通过 JAK/STAT 通路来 “打开” 炎症相关基因。

When a cytokine binds its receptor, JAK activates STAT, and STAT goes into the nucleus to change gene expression.当细胞因子结合受体时，JAK 激活 STAT，STAT 进入细胞核并改变基因表达。

JAK inhibitors block this pathway for several pro-inflammatory cytokines at the same time.JAK 抑制剂可以同时阻断多种促炎细胞因子的这条信号通路。

As a result, Th1/Th17 responses go down, immune balance is partly restored, and gut inflammation is reduced.结果是 Th1/Th17 反应下降，免疫平衡部分恢复，肠道炎症减轻。

02病理生理总复习（双语）

Physiopathology Exam Master Rescue Notes

病理生理终极救命重点（双语版）

Excluding the separate paper/figure-interpretation guide | 不含单独的论文/图表解读指南

Use this file as the main memorization backbone: memorize the Scoring Answers 得分答案 first, then read the Understanding 理解性讲解 to connect mechanisms.

How to Use This File / 使用方法

• Step 1
Memorize the Scoring Answers 得分答案 aloud until you can say them without notes. / 先把得分答案大声背到能脱口而出。
• Step 2
Read the Understanding 理解性讲解 only to connect the mechanism, not to drown in details. / 再看理解性讲解，目的是把逻辑串起来，不是陷进细节。
• Step 3
Rehearse with arrows: trigger → mechanism → consequence. / 最后一定用箭头练：诱因 → 机制 → 结果。
• Priority order
Cardiac Ca2+ & HF → Endothelium → Mucosal immunology → Pharmacology → High-yield extras. / 复习优先级：心脏钙与心衰 → 内皮 → 黏膜免疫 → 药理 → 高频补充。

Section 1 – Cardiac Ca2+ Signaling, Ischemia, Mitochondria, and Heart Failure / 第一部分：心肌钙信号、缺血、线粒体与心衰

1. Excitation–Contraction Coupling (ECC) / 兴奋-收缩耦合

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Cardiac contraction depends on calcium-induced calcium release. / 心肌收缩依赖钙诱导的钙释放。

• Action potential → L-type Ca2+ channel opening → small Ca2+ influx → RyR2 opening → SR Ca2+ release → troponin C binding → contraction. / 动作电位 → L型钙通道开放 → 少量Ca2+内流 → RyR2开放 → 肌浆网释放Ca2+ → 肌钙蛋白C结合 → 收缩。

• Relaxation depends on SERCA reuptake and NCX extrusion. / 舒张依赖SERCA回收Ca2+和NCX外排Ca2+。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
ECC is the normal backbone of every later pathology question. CN: 先把正常链条记牢，后面所有异常题都是在这个基础上变坏。
• EN
The key idea is that SR Ca2+ content determines contraction strength. CN: 核心思想就是肌浆网钙库决定下一拍收缩强度。

2. Ca2+ Dysregulation in Heart Failure / 心衰中的钙失衡

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• SERCA↓ → SR Ca2+ reuptake↓ → SR Ca2+ content↓ → systolic Ca2+ release↓ → contractility↓. / SERCA下降 → 肌浆网回收Ca2+下降 → 钙库下降 → 收缩期Ca2+释放下降 → 收缩力下降。

• NCX↑ can further deplete SR Ca2+ stores. / NCX增高会进一步耗空肌浆网钙库。

• RyR leak↑ → diastolic Ca2+↑ → arrhythmia risk↑. / RyR漏钙增加 → 舒张期Ca2+升高 → 心律失常风险升高。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
In heart failure, calcium handling is not only weaker but also unstable. CN: 心衰里的钙处理不是单纯“变弱”，而是同时“变乱”。
• EN
SERCA explains weak contraction, while RyR leak explains arrhythmia. CN: SERCA负责解释收缩差，RyR漏钙负责解释为什么容易心律失常。

3. Why SERCA Reduction Weakens Contraction / 为什么SERCA下降会让收缩变差

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Reduced SERCA activity lowers SR Ca2+ reuptake, depletes SR Ca2+ stores, and reduces the next systolic Ca2+ release. / SERCA活性下降会降低肌浆网回收Ca2+，耗空钙库，并减少下一次收缩期的Ca2+释放。

• Less Ca2+ reaches troponin C, so contraction becomes weaker. / 到达肌钙蛋白C的Ca2+变少，因此收缩减弱。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
The exam often asks this as a chain question. CN: 这道很常以箭头链形式单独出题。
• EN
One-line memory: SERCA↓ → SR empty → low Ca2+ peak → weak contraction. CN: 一句话记忆：SERCA↓ → 钙库空 → 钙峰低 → 收缩弱。

4. Why RyR Leak Promotes Arrhythmia / 为什么RyR漏钙会导致心律失常

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• RyR leak↑ → diastolic intracellular Ca2+↑ → NCX overactivation → inward depolarizing current → DAD → arrhythmia. / RyR漏钙增加 → 舒张期胞内Ca2+升高 → NCX过度激活 → 内向去极化电流 → DAD → 心律失常。

• NCX exchanges 1 Ca2+ out for 3 Na+ in, producing a depolarizing current. / NCX以1个Ca2+外排换3个Na+内流，因此会产生去极化电流。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is the key bridge many students miss. CN: 这是很多人最容易断掉的一座桥。
• EN
Diastolic Ca2+ itself is not the final electrical event; NCX-mediated inward current is what triggers DADs. CN: 真正触发DAD的不是“舒张钙高”本身，而是NCX造成的内向电流。

5. CaMKII vs Calcineurin–NFAT / CaMKII 与 Calcineurin–NFAT 对比

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• CaMKII is a Ca2+/calmodulin-dependent kinase activated mainly by Ca2+ oscillations; it phosphorylates ion-handling proteins and promotes electrical remodeling and arrhythmia. / CaMKII是由Ca2+/钙调蛋白激活的激酶，主要响应Ca2+波动，通过磷酸化离子处理蛋白促进电重构和心律失常。

• Calcineurin is a Ca2+-dependent phosphatase activated by sustained Ca2+ elevation; it dephosphorylates NFAT and promotes pathological hypertrophy. / Calcineurin是Ca2+依赖性磷酸酶，主要响应持续Ca2+升高，通过去磷酸化NFAT促进病理性肥厚。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
Use this comparison when a question asks signaling pathways downstream of calcium. CN: 只要题目问“钙下游信号通路比较”，这组对比几乎必用。
• EN
Quick distinction: CaMKII = arrhythmia/electrical instability; calcineurin = hypertrophy/gene reprogramming. CN: 快速区分：CaMKII偏心律失常，calcineurin偏肥厚和转录重编程。

6. Ischemia, Reperfusion, and Irreversible Injury / 缺血、再灌注与不可逆损伤

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Ischemia → O2↓ → oxidative phosphorylation↓ → ATP↓ → ion pump failure → intracellular Na+↑ / Ca2+ overload↑. / 缺血 → 氧下降 → 氧化磷酸化下降 → ATP下降 → 离子泵失效 → 细胞内Na+升高 / Ca2+超载。

• Prolonged ischemia causes mitochondrial dysfunction, membrane damage, and irreversible injury. / 持续缺血会导致线粒体功能障碍、膜损伤和不可逆损伤。

• Reperfusion can worsen injury through ROS burst, Ca2+ overload, and inflammation. / 再灌注会通过ROS爆发、Ca2+超载和炎症反应进一步加重损伤。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
Reperfusion is necessary, but it is not purely beneficial. CN: 再灌注是必要的，但不是“只有好处”。
• EN
The exam likes the contrast between reversible injury and irreversible injury. CN: 常考点是可逆损伤与不可逆损伤的对比。

7. Mitochondria, ATP Failure, and the Energy–Ca2+ Vicious Cycle / 线粒体、ATP衰竭与能量-钙恶性循环

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Mitochondrial dysfunction → ATP↓ → SERCA dysfunction → Ca2+ overload worsens → mitochondrial stress↑ → ROS↑. / 线粒体功能障碍 → ATP下降 → SERCA功能变差 → Ca2+超载加重 → 线粒体应激上升 → ROS增加。

• PGC-1α is the master regulator of mitochondrial biogenesis. / PGC-1α是线粒体生物发生的主调控因子。

• Mitophagy removes damaged mitochondria; impaired mitophagy increases ROS and energy failure. / 线粒体自噬负责清除受损线粒体；其受损会增加ROS并加重能量衰竭。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
Day 4 should not be memorized as isolated biochemistry. CN: 第4天不要背成孤立的生化知识。
• EN
The only exam-level idea you must own is the energy failure ↔ Ca2+ dysregulation vicious cycle. CN: 真正要拿住的是能量衰竭与钙失衡互相放大这个恶性循环。

8. Full Heart Failure Progression Chain / 从缺血到心衰的总链

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Ischemia → ATP depletion → Ca2+ overload → mitochondrial dysfunction → energy failure → CaMKII & calcineurin activation → remodeling/fibrosis → heart failure. / 缺血 → ATP耗竭 → Ca2+超载 → 线粒体功能障碍 → 能量衰竭 → CaMKII与calcineurin激活 → 重构/纤维化 → 心衰。

• Contractile dysfunction, arrhythmia, hypertrophy, and fibrosis are all linked by chronic Ca2+ and energy failure. / 收缩功能障碍、心律失常、肥厚和纤维化都由慢性钙失衡与能量衰竭串联起来。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is the integrated answer you need when the question is broad. CN: 这就是“综合性大题”最该写出的主干。
• EN
Day 8 is not new knowledge; it is the stitched summary of Days 1–4. CN: Day 8不是新知识，而是Day 1–4的缝合总图。

Section 2 – Endothelium and Vascular Activation / 第二部分：血管内皮与血管激活

1. Resting vs Activated Endothelium / 静息内皮与激活内皮

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Resting endothelium is anti-inflammatory, anti-thrombotic, and low-permeability. / 静息内皮具有抗炎、抗凝和低通透性特点。

• Activated endothelium is pro-inflammatory, pro-thrombotic, and hyperpermeable. / 激活内皮具有促炎、促凝和高通透性特点。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This comparison is one of the cleanest scoring questions in the vascular block. CN: 这是血管部分最稳定、最容易拿分的对比题之一。
• EN
If the question mentions microvascular endothelial activation, immediately think of inflammation + permeability + thrombosis. CN: 看到“微血管内皮激活”，脑子里立刻出现炎症 + 通透性 + 血栓形成。

2. Three Cardinal Features of Endothelial Activation / 内皮激活的三大特征

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Vascular inflammation: adhesion molecules and chemokines recruit leukocytes. / 血管炎症：黏附分子与趋化因子招募白细胞。

• Vascular permeability: endothelial junctions open and plasma proteins leak out. / 血管通透性：内皮连接打开，血浆蛋白外渗。

• Thrombus formation: vWF, P-selectin, and tissue factor support platelet adhesion and coagulation. / 血栓形成：vWF、P-selectin和组织因子促进血小板黏附和凝血。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This wording mirrors the teachers' own framing. CN: 这一组正是老师题目里最核心的表述方式。
• EN
Always separate the answer into three subparts instead of writing one long paragraph. CN: 做题时一定分成三小段，不要糊成一大段。

3. Leukocyte Adhesion Cascade / 白细胞黏附级联

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Rolling
selectins mediate weak leukocyte tethering. / 滚动：selectin介导弱黏附。
• Activation
chemokines activate leukocyte integrins. / 激活：趋化因子激活白细胞整合素。
• Firm adhesion
ICAM/VCAM bind activated integrins. / 牢固黏附：ICAM/VCAM与活化整合素结合。

• Diapedesis/transmigration: leukocytes cross the endothelial barrier. / 穿越：白细胞穿过内皮屏障。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
The exam often rewards correct order more than excessive detail. CN: 这道题通常“顺序对”比“细节多”更值钱。
• EN
Think sequence, not isolated molecules. CN: 记的是流程，不是零散分子。

4. Weibel–Palade Bodies (WPB) / Weibel–Palade 小体

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• WPB store vWF, P-selectin, IL-8, and Ang2. / WPB储存vWF、P-selectin、IL-8和Ang2。

• Rapid WPB release converts resting endothelium into a pro-inflammatory and pro-thrombotic surface. / WPB快速释放会把静息内皮转变成促炎、促凝表面。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
WPB are the fast-response granules of endothelial activation. CN: WPB就是内皮激活时的“快速应急颗粒”。
• EN
When you see vWF + P-selectin + Ang2, think WPB immediately. CN: 看到vWF + P-selectin + Ang2，立刻联想到WPB。

5. Tie2–Ang1–Ang2 Axis / Tie2–Ang1–Ang2轴

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Ang1 activates Tie2 and maintains endothelial quiescence, barrier stability, and anti-inflammatory tone. / Ang1激活Tie2，维持内皮静息、屏障稳定和抗炎状态。

• Ang2 antagonizes/destabilizes Tie2 signaling and promotes permeability and vascular activation. / Ang2拮抗/破坏Tie2信号，促进通透性升高和血管激活。

• Ang1 is produced mainly by mural cells/pericytes. / Ang1主要由壁细胞/周细胞产生。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is the classic answer for endothelial quiescence. CN: 这是“内皮静息维持”最标准的一组答案。
• EN
Tie2 questions are often easier if you compare stabilizing Ang1 with destabilizing Ang2. CN: 回答Tie2时最稳妥的方式是直接对比稳定内皮的Ang1和破坏稳定的Ang2。

6. Histamine and Vascular Permeability / 组胺与血管通透性

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Histamine increases vascular permeability by disrupting endothelial junctions and promoting endothelial contraction. / 组胺通过破坏内皮连接并促进内皮收缩来增加血管通透性。

• Barrier molecules include adherens and tight junction proteins such as VE-cadherin. / 屏障分子包括黏附连接和紧密连接蛋白，如VE-cadherin。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
If a figure shows leakage of fluorescent dextran, the core answer is barrier breakdown + extravasation. CN: 如果图里看到荧光大分子外漏，核心就是屏障破坏 + 外渗。
• EN
Even if you forget the receptor details, write junction opening and endothelial contraction. CN: 就算忘了受体，也一定写连接打开和内皮收缩。

Section 3 – Mucosal Immunology / 第三部分：黏膜免疫

1. Type I vs Type II Mucosa / Type I 与 Type II 黏膜

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Type I mucosa
simple columnar epithelium, e.g. gut and respiratory tract; IgA-dominant; more tolerance-oriented. / Type I黏膜：单层柱状上皮，如肠道和呼吸道；以IgA为主；更偏耐受。
• Type II mucosa
stratified squamous epithelium, e.g. oral cavity and similar protective surfaces; more inflammatory protection; more IgG-oriented. / Type II黏膜：复层鳞状上皮，如口腔等保护性表面；更偏炎症性防御；更偏IgG。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
The exam wants both the structure and the immune logic. CN: 这题既要写上皮结构，也要写免疫学意义。
• EN
Type I = thin surface needing tolerance; Type II = tougher surface needing mechanical and inflammatory protection. CN: Type I薄、强调耐受；Type II厚、强调机械和炎症防御。

2. Main Cell Types in Gut Epithelium / 肠上皮主要细胞

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Enterocytes
absorption and barrier. / 吸收细胞：吸收与屏障。
• Goblet cells
mucus production. / 杯状细胞：分泌黏液。
• Paneth cells
antimicrobial peptides. / 潘氏细胞：分泌抗菌肽。
• M cells
antigen sampling and initiation of mucosal immune responses. / M细胞：取样抗原并启动黏膜免疫。

• Enteroendocrine cells (plus intraepithelial immune surveillance as an added point): hormone signaling. / 肠内分泌细胞（可加写上皮内免疫监视）: 激素信号。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
If the teacher follows the summary PDF closely, adding intraepithelial CD8+ cells as a surveillance point is smart. CN: 如果贴老师summary，更稳的是补一句上皮内CD8+细胞负责免疫监视。
• EN
Do not just list cells; add one function per cell. CN: 不能只列名字，每个细胞后面至少跟一个功能。

3. Secretory IgA and Gut Microbiota / 分泌型IgA与肠道菌群

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• IgA is the main immunoglobulin controlling gut flora. / IgA是控制肠道菌群的主要免疫球蛋白。

• sIgA shapes microbiota through immune exclusion, agglutination, and enchained growth. / sIgA通过免疫排斥、凝集和链式生长限制来塑造菌群。

• It prevents pathogenic colonization, promotes beneficial colonization, and can influence bacterial behavior. / 它能阻止致病菌定植、促进有益菌定植，并影响细菌行为。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is one of the most likely short-answer questions. CN: 这是极高频的短答题。
• EN
The key point is that IgA shapes the microbiota without creating destructive inflammation. CN: 核心是IgA在不引发强破坏性炎症的情况下塑造菌群。

4. Three Signals for Full T-cell Activation / T细胞完全激活的三个信号

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Signal 1
TCR recognition of peptide–MHC. / 信号1：TCR识别肽-MHC。
• Signal 2
co-stimulation, classically CD80/86–CD28. / 信号2：共刺激，经典为CD80/86–CD28。
• Signal 3
cytokine environment driving differentiation and expansion. / 信号3：细胞因子环境驱动分化与扩增。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
Many mucosal and IBD questions are built on this model. CN: 很多黏膜免疫和IBD题都以这套三信号模型为底。
• EN
If one of the signals is weak or absent, the outcome can shift from activation to tolerance. CN: 如果某一信号弱或缺失，结果就可能从激活转向耐受。

5. Commensals vs Pathogens; Th17 vs Treg; IBD / 共生菌与致病菌；Th17与Treg；IBD

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Commensal bacteria favor tolerogenic dendritic cells, weak activation signals, IL-10/retinoic acid production, and Treg dominance. / 共生菌促进耐受性树突细胞、弱激活信号、IL-10/维甲酸产生，以及Treg占优势。

• Pathogenic bacteria induce pro-inflammatory dendritic cells, strong 3-signal activation, and Th17-type inflammation. / 致病菌诱导促炎性树突细胞、强三信号激活，以及Th17型炎症。

• IBD reflects loss of mucus barrier, dysbiosis, and a shift from Treg balance toward Th17-driven inflammation. / IBD本质上是黏液屏障丢失、菌群失衡以及从Treg平衡转向Th17驱动炎症。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This topic links microbiota, epithelial barrier, cytokines, and disease. CN: 这块把菌群、屏障、细胞因子和疾病都串起来了。
• EN
The teacher summary explicitly frames IBD as a Th17/Treg imbalance. CN: 老师给的重点里明确把IBD框成Th17/Treg失衡。

6. JAK/STAT and IBD Therapy / JAK/STAT 与 IBD治疗

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• JAK/STAT transduces cytokine-receptor signals to the nucleus. / JAK/STAT负责把细胞因子受体信号传到细胞核。

• JAK inhibitors reduce inflammatory cytokine signaling and therefore mainly target Signal 3. / JAK抑制剂降低炎症性细胞因子信号，因此主要作用于信号3。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
If the exam asks for an IBD drug targeting one of the three activation signals, this is the safest answer. CN: 如果题目问“IBD药物针对三信号中的哪一个”，这就是最稳答案。
• EN
Keep it simple: cytokine pathway blockade = signal 3 targeting. CN: 就记一句：阻断细胞因子通路 = 针对信号3。

Section 4 – Cardiovascular Pharmacology / 第四部分：心血管药理

1. α1 vs α2 Adrenergic Receptors / α1 与 α2 肾上腺素能受体

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• α1 is Gq-coupled
PLC → IP3/DAG → Ca2+↑ → smooth muscle contraction → vasoconstriction. / α1是Gq偶联：PLC → IP3/DAG → Ca2+升高 → 平滑肌收缩 → 血管收缩。
• α2 is Gi-coupled
adenylyl cyclase↓ → cAMP↓ → norepinephrine release↓ → sympathetic tone↓. / α2是Gi偶联：腺苷酸环化酶下降 → cAMP下降 → 去甲肾上腺素释放下降 → 交感张力下降。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
α1 is the vessel-constricting receptor; α2 is the sympathetic brake. CN: α1是收缩血管的，α2更像交感神经的“刹车”。
• EN
In short questions, always mention the G protein + final tissue effect. CN: 短答题最稳写法是同时给出G蛋白类型 + 最终组织效应。

2. β1 vs β2 Adrenergic Receptors / β1 与 β2 肾上腺素能受体

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• β1 is Gs-coupled in the heart: cAMP/PKA↑ → L-type Ca2+ current↑ → heart rate and contractility↑. / β1是心脏中的Gs偶联受体：cAMP/PKA升高 → L型Ca2+电流增加 → 心率和收缩力升高。

• β2 mainly promotes smooth muscle relaxation such as vasodilation and bronchodilation. / β2主要促进平滑肌舒张，如血管舒张和支气管舒张。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
If you compare β1 and β2, the easiest divide is heart stimulation versus smooth-muscle relaxation. CN: 对比β1和β2时，最简洁的分法是心脏兴奋与平滑肌舒张。
• EN
Chronic β1 overstimulation contributes to Ca2+ overload and heart failure progression. CN: 慢性β1过度刺激会促成Ca2+超载并推动心衰进展。

3. ACE Inhibitors vs ARBs / ACE抑制剂与ARB

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• ACE inhibitors reduce Ang II formation and increase bradykinin, causing vasodilation and lower blood pressure. / ACE抑制剂降低Ang II形成并增加缓激肽，从而引起血管舒张和降压。

• ARBs block the AT1 receptor, reducing Ang II effects without increasing bradykinin. / ARB阻断AT1受体，减少Ang II作用，但不增加缓激肽。

• Both reduce afterload and remodeling; ACEI has more cough/angioedema risk because of bradykinin. / 两者都降低后负荷并减少重构；ACEI因缓激肽升高而更容易咳嗽/血管性水肿。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is exactly the type of mechanism the teacher said to explain. CN: 这正是老师口头强调要会解释的机制型药理题。
• EN
One-line memory: ACEI = Ang II↓ + bradykinin↑; ARB = block AT1, no bradykinin effect. CN: 一句话记忆：ACEI = Ang II↓ + bradykinin↑；ARB = 阻断AT1，不影响缓激肽。

4. Calcium Channel Blockers and Verapamil / 钙通道阻滞剂与维拉帕米

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• CCBs target L-type Ca2+ channels. / 钙通道阻滞剂作用于L型Ca2+通道。

• Blocking L-type Ca2+ entry reduces vascular smooth muscle contraction and lowers blood pressure. / 阻断L型Ca2+内流会降低血管平滑肌收缩并降压。

• Verapamil also reduces AV conduction and contractility. / 维拉帕米还会降低房室传导和心肌收缩力。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
When the teacher mentions calcium pharmacology, this is one of the safest molecules to discuss. CN: 老师提到“钙相关药理”时，这是一种最稳的分子。
• EN
Non-dihydropyridines are more cardiac; dihydropyridines are more vascular. CN: 非二氢吡啶类更偏心脏，二氢吡啶类更偏血管。

5. Enalapril and Verapamil Table Logic / Enalapril 与 Verapamil 表格题写法

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Always answer in four layers: target → normal physiological role → pharmacodynamic effects → clinical benefit. / 表格题永远按四层写：靶点 → 正常生理作用 → 药效学作用 → 临床获益。

• Enalapril
ACE target; Ang II↓/aldosterone↓; vasodilation and lower preload/afterload; limits remodeling. / Enalapril：靶点是ACE；Ang II和醛固酮下降；血管舒张、前后负荷下降；限制重构。
• Verapamil
L-type Ca2+ channel target; Ca2+ entry↓; AV conduction↓ and contractility↓; useful in hypertension/arrhythmias. / Verapamil：靶点是L型Ca2+通道；Ca2+内流下降；房室传导和收缩力下降；可用于高血压/心律失常。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
The biggest mistake is naming the class without completing the chain. CN: 最大失误是只写药物类别，不把后面机制和意义写全。
• EN
In this course, drug questions are mechanism chains, not mere definitions. CN: 这门课里的药理题本质是机制链，不是定义题。

Section 5 – High-Yield Extras from the Previous Exam / 第五部分：往年试卷中的高频补充点

1. RyR2 Regulatory Proteins / RyR2 调控蛋白

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• Cytosolic regulators: calmodulin, FKBP12.6. / 胞质侧调控：calmodulin、FKBP12.6。

• Luminal regulators: calsequestrin, triadin, junctin. / 腔内侧调控：calsequestrin、triadin、junctin。

• These proteins stabilize RyR2, sense SR Ca2+, and limit abnormal leak. / 这些蛋白负责稳定RyR2、感受肌浆网Ca2+并限制异常漏钙。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is a classic ventricular cardiomyocyte question. CN: 这是非常经典的心室肌细胞题。
• EN
If you forget details, at least divide the answer into cytosolic and luminal. CN: 就算忘细节，也至少分成胞质侧和腔内侧来写。

2. Sinoatrial Node vs Ventricular Myocyte / 窦房结细胞与心室肌细胞

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• SA node cells fire spontaneously because they have funny current (If), weak IK1, and unstable diastolic depolarization. / 窦房结细胞能自发起搏，因为它有If电流、IK1弱、舒张期去极化不稳定。

• Ventricular myocytes do not fire spontaneously because strong IK1 stabilizes the resting membrane potential. / 心室肌细胞不会自发起搏，因为强IK1维持了稳定的静息膜电位。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This is a comparison question, so always answer both cell types. CN: 这是标准对比题，一定两边都写。
• EN
The real distinction is unstable pacemaker potential versus stable resting potential. CN: 真正的核心区别是不稳定起搏电位与稳定静息电位。

3. OPA1 and Mitochondrial Morphology / OPA1 与线粒体形态

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• OPA1 is involved in inner mitochondrial membrane fusion and cristae organization. / OPA1参与线粒体内膜融合和嵴结构维持。

• Reduced OPA1 leads to smaller/more fragmented mitochondria and impaired mitochondrial integrity. / OPA1减少会导致线粒体更小/更碎，并损害线粒体完整性。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
Even outside figure questions, this is a useful mitochondria concept. CN: 即使不讨论看图技巧，这也是一个高价值的线粒体概念。
• EN
Keep the answer broad: fusion + cristae + energy integrity. CN: 这题最稳就是写融合 + 嵴结构 + 能量完整性。

4. SGLT2 Inhibitors / SGLT2 抑制剂

Scoring Answers / 得分答案

• SGLT2 inhibition causes glucosuria, natriuresis, and osmotic diuresis. / SGLT2抑制会导致糖尿、排钠和渗透性利尿。

• Renal benefit goes beyond glucose lowering: restored tubuloglomerular feedback and reduced intraglomerular pressure. / 肾保护不只来自降糖，还包括恢复管球反馈和降低肾小球内压。

• In AKI or hypovolemia, volume depletion is a major risk. / 在急性肾损伤或低血容量时，容量不足是主要风险。

Understanding / 理解性讲解

• EN
This topic combines renal physiology and pharmacology. CN: 这题把肾脏生理和药理放在一起考。
• EN
If asked about the figure, the classic line is small early dip, better long-term protection. CN: 如果结合图形，经典表述就是早期轻降，长期保护更好。

Final 12 Must-Say Sentences / 最后12句保命句

Rapid Memorization / 临门一脚背诵

• Cardiac contraction depends on calcium-induced calcium release. / 心肌收缩依赖钙诱导的钙释放。

• Reduced SERCA activity depletes SR Ca2+ stores and weakens contraction. / SERCA活性下降会耗空肌浆网钙库并削弱收缩。

• RyR leak increases diastolic Ca2+ and promotes arrhythmia through NCX-dependent DADs. / RyR漏钙通过NCX依赖的DAD增加舒张钙并促进心律失常。

• CaMKII mainly drives electrical remodeling and arrhythmia, whereas calcineurin–NFAT drives pathological hypertrophy. / CaMKII主要推动电重构与心律失常，而calcineurin–NFAT推动病理性肥厚。

• Ischemia reduces ATP production, causing pump failure, Ca2+ overload, mitochondrial dysfunction, and eventually heart failure. / 缺血会减少ATP生成，导致离子泵失效、Ca2+超载、线粒体障碍，最终走向心衰。

• Resting endothelium is anti-inflammatory, anti-thrombotic, and barrier-protective, whereas activated endothelium is pro-inflammatory, pro-thrombotic, and hyperpermeable. / 静息内皮抗炎、抗凝、保护屏障，而激活内皮促炎、促凝且高通透。

• Histamine increases vascular permeability by disrupting endothelial junctions and promoting endothelial contraction. / 组胺通过破坏内皮连接并促进内皮收缩增加血管通透性。

• Ang1 activates Tie2 to stabilize the endothelium, whereas Ang2 promotes vascular activation. / Ang1激活Tie2稳定内皮，而Ang2促进血管激活。

• Type I mucosa is IgA-dominant and tolerance-oriented, whereas Type II mucosa is more inflammatory and IgG-oriented. / Type I黏膜以IgA和耐受为主，而Type II更偏炎症防御和IgG。

• Secretory IgA shapes gut microbiota through immune exclusion, agglutination, and enchained growth. / 分泌型IgA通过免疫排斥、凝集和链式生长限制塑造肠道菌群。

• Full T-cell activation requires antigen recognition, co-stimulation, and cytokine signaling. / T细胞完全激活需要抗原识别、共刺激和细胞因子信号。

• ACE inhibitors reduce angiotensin II formation and increase bradykinin, leading to vasodilation and lower blood pressure. / ACE抑制剂降低Ang II形成并增加缓激肽，从而引起血管舒张和降压。

This document intentionally omits the separate paper/figure-interpretation method, which is covered in another file. / 本文档刻意不包含单独的论文/图表解读方法，那部分已在另一份文件中单独整理。

03免疫核心机制（D1–D2）

D1–D2 Immunology Core Mechanism Revision (Exam-Oriented Version)

This document is intentionally structured to be sufficient for D1–D2 revision. If you master everything here (mechanisms + comparisons + logic chains), you are fully prepared for immunology-related exam questions.

PART I – Core Mechanism Chains (Must Be Memorized)

1. Global Immune Response Cascade

Pathogen

→ Innate sensing (PRRs on dendritic cells/macrophages)

→ 1st order cytokines (e.g., IL-12, IL-1, TNF)

→ Lymphocyte activation

→ 2nd order cytokines (e.g., IFN-γ, IL-4, IL-21)

→ Effector response (macrophage activation / antibody production / cytotoxic killing)

→ Resolution or Tolerance

Key principle
Immune response must be activated AND terminated to avoid chronic inflammation.

2. 1st vs 2nd Order Cytokines

1st order cytokines

• Produced by innate immune cells

• Activate lymphocytes

• Drive T cell differentiation

• Shape immune response

2nd order cytokines

• Produced by activated lymphocytes

• Execute effector functions

• Recruit immune cells

• Promote class switching

Mechanism example:

Pathogen → DC sensing → IL-12 (1st) → T cell activation → IFN-γ (2nd) → macrophage activation

3. T Cell Activation – Three Signal Model

Signal 1
MHC–TCR recognition
Signal 2
CD80/86–CD28 co-stimulation
Signal 3
Cytokine environment (drives differentiation)

Critical mechanism:

Only Signal 1 → No co-stimulation → Anergy / apoptosis → Peripheral tolerance

Exam explanation line:

Absence of costimulation prevents autoimmunity by inducing functional inactivation of self-reactive T cells.

4. T-Dependent vs T-Independent Antibody Responses

T-Dependent (Protein antigen)

→ MHC II presentation

→ Tfh help

→ CD40–CD40L interaction

→ IL-21 secretion

→ AID activation

→ Class switching

→ Affinity maturation

→ Memory B cells

T-Independent (Polysaccharide/lipid)

→ No MHC II presentation

→ Direct B cell activation

→ IgM only

→ No memory

问Why higher affinity in T-dependent?
答

Because germinal center reaction + somatic hypermutation.

5. Germinal Center Reaction

B cell + Tfh interaction

→ CD40–CD40L

→ AID activation

→ Somatic hypermutation

→ Selection on FDC

→ High-affinity B cells survive

→ Memory B cells + Long-lived plasma cells

6. Long-Lived Plasma Cells

Germinal center reaction

→ Plasma cells migrate to bone marrow

→ Continuous IgG secretion for years

Exam logic:

Persistent antibody levels are due to long-lived plasma cells in bone marrow.

7. Central vs Peripheral Tolerance

Central tolerance (Thymus)

→ Strong self-antigen recognition

→ Negative selection (apoptosis)

Peripheral tolerance

→ Anergy (Signal 1 only)

→ Deletion

→ Treg suppression

Affinity rule:

Strong affinity → deletion

Moderate affinity → Treg differentiation

8. AIRE Mechanism

mTEC express peripheral tissue antigens via AIRE

→ Self-reactive T cells deleted in thymus

AIRE defect

→ Failure of central tolerance

→ Autoimmune disease

9. Regulatory T Cells (Treg)

Markers
FOXP3, CD25, CTLA-4

Mechanisms:

• CTLA-4 blocks CD80/86 (inhibits costimulation)

• Secrete anti-inflammatory cytokines

• Suppress effector T cell proliferation

PART II – Must Understand (Logical Structure)

• Lymphocyte circulation: Blood → Lymph node → Efferent lymph → Thoracic duct → Blood

• Naive T cell entry: Rolling → Activation → Adhesion → Diapedesis

• High-affinity B cell selection: Competition on FDC → Survival vs Apoptosis

• Effector antibody functions: Neutralization / Opsonization / Complement / ADCC

PART III – Self-Test Questions (You Must Answer Without Notes)

问1. Why does absence of costimulation induce tolerance?
问2. Why do T-dependent responses generate memory and high-affinity antibodies?
问3. Why does AIRE mutation cause autoimmune disease?
问4. Why does T-independent response produce only IgM?
答
5
Explain the full immune cascade from pathogen recognition to resolution.

If you can answer these clearly in 6–8 structured lines each, you have mastered D1–D2 immunology for the exam.

04Day 1 心肌 Ca²⁺ 核心机制

Day 1 – Cardiac Ca2+ Core Mechanisms (Exam-Oriented Version)

This document is strictly structured according to the top student's logic framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based exam questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Cardiac Ca2+ module.

PART I – Excitation–Contraction Coupling (ECC) 兴奋-收缩耦合（ECC）

Normal Mechanism Chain:

Action potential

→ L-type Ca2+ channel opening

→ Small Ca2+ influx

→ RyR2 activation (SR release)

→ Cytosolic Ca2+ increase

→ Troponin C binding

→ Contraction

Relaxation:

SERCA (SR Ca2+ ATPase) → Ca2+ reuptake into SR

NCX (Na+/Ca2+ exchanger) → Ca2+ extrusion

Key concept:

CICR (Calcium-induced calcium release)

Small Ca2+ influx triggers massive SR Ca2+ release.

PART II – Ca2+ Dysregulation in Heart Failure

Key Changes in HF:

SERCA ↓

→ Reduced Ca2+ reuptake

→ SR Ca2+ content ↓

→ Systolic Ca2+ release ↓

→ Contractility ↓

NCX ↑

→ Increased Ca2+ extrusion

→ Further SR depletion

RyR leak ↑

→ Diastolic Ca2+ elevation

→ Arrhythmia risk ↑

Core Summary:

Impaired reuptake + Increased leak

= Reduced contractility + Increased arrhythmia

PART III – CaMKII Pathway

Activation:

Ca2+ ↑

→ Calmodulin binding

→ CaMKII activation

Targets:

• RyR phosphorylation → Ca2+ leak ↑

• L-type Ca channel modulation

• Phospholamban (PLN) regulation

Pathological Effect:

Chronic CaMKII activation

→ Gene expression changes

→ Hypertrophy

→ Arrhythmia

→ HF progression

PART IV – Calcineurin–NFAT Pathway

Calcineurin = Ca2+-dependent phosphatase

Mechanism:

Sustained Ca2+ elevation

→ Calcineurin activation

→ NFAT dephosphorylation

→ NFAT nuclear translocation

→ Fetal gene expression

→ Pathological hypertrophy

Comparison:

CaMKII = kinase, fast oscillatory Ca2+, arrhythmia dominant

Calcineurin = phosphatase, sustained Ca2+, hypertrophy gene program

PART V – EPAC Pathway

Beta-adrenergic stimulation

→ cAMP increase

Two pathways:

PKA → Acute contractility enhancement

EPAC → Rap1 activation → Remodeling signaling

Exam concept:

PKA = short-term functional modulation

EPAC = long-term remodeling

INTEGRATION – Why Ca2+ Dysregulation Causes Heart Failure

Chronic Ca2+ overload

→ SERCA dysfunction

→ SR Ca2+ depletion

→ Reduced contractility

Simultaneously:

→ CaMKII activation

→ RyR leak

→ Arrhythmia

Sustained Ca2+ elevation

→ Calcineurin activation

→ NFAT-mediated gene transcription

→ Pathological hypertrophy

Increased energy demand

→ Mitochondrial stress

→ HF progression

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare CaMKII vs Calcineurin.
2
Explain how SERCA reduction decreases contractility.
3
Explain why RyR leak increases arrhythmia risk.
4
Explain how chronic beta-adrenergic stimulation promotes remodeling.

05Day 2 心肌肥厚与重构

Day 2 – Cardiac Hypertrophy & Remodeling (Exam-Oriented Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's notes. It is optimized for mechanism-based comparison and explanation questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Hypertrophy & Remodeling module.

PART I – Physiological vs Pathological Hypertrophy

Physiological Hypertrophy (生理性肥厚):

• Trigger
Exercise / pregnancy

• Adaptive remodeling

• Preserved contractility

• No fibrosis

• Reversible

• Normal gene expression pattern

Pathological Hypertrophy (病理性肥厚):

• Trigger
Chronic pressure overload / neurohormonal activation

• Maladaptive remodeling

• Contractile dysfunction

• Fibrosis present

• Progresses to heart failure

• Fetal gene reprogramming

Key Comparison Logic:

Physiological = functional adaptation

Pathological = structural + molecular maladaptation

PART II – CaMKII vs Calcineurin Signaling

PART II – CaMKII vs Calcineurin（考试够用版本）

1) CaMKII pathway（偏心律失常 / electrical remodeling）

Ca²⁺ oscillations（快速波动）

→ Ca²⁺–calmodulin

→ CaMKII activation (kinase, phosphorylation)

→ RyR phosphorylation → SR Ca²⁺ leak ↑

→ L-type Ca²⁺ channel modulation → Ca²⁺ influx ↑

→ intracellular Ca²⁺ instability

→ arrhythmia risk ↑ + remodeling progression

2) Calcineurin–NFAT pathway（偏肥厚 / gene remodeling）

Sustained Ca²⁺ elevation（持续升高）

→ Ca²⁺–calmodulin

→ Calcineurin activation (phosphatase, dephosphorylation)

→ NFAT dephosphorylation

→ NFAT nuclear translocation

→ fetal gene program ↑

→ pathological hypertrophy → HF progression

3) Compare（直接写作模板，2–4 分题够用）

CaMKII is a Ca²⁺/calmodulin-dependent kinase activated mainly by Ca²⁺ oscillations; it phosphorylates ion-handling proteins (RyR, L-type channels), causing Ca²⁺ instability and arrhythmia-dominant remodeling.

Calcineurin is a Ca²⁺-dependent phosphatase activated by sustained Ca²⁺ elevation; it activates NFAT-mediated transcription, inducing the fetal gene program and pathological hypertrophy.

PART III – Gene Expression Reprogramming

Chronic Ca2+ signaling

→ Activation of transcription factors (NFAT, MEF2)

→ Fetal gene expression pattern

→ Shift in contractile protein isoforms

→ Reduced contractile efficiency

→ Structural remodeling

Core Concept:

Hypertrophy is not just cell enlargement.

It is transcriptional reprogramming (基因表达重编程).

PART IV – Fibrosis Mechanism

Chronic stress

→ Fibroblast activation

→ Collagen deposition

→ Extracellular matrix remodeling

→ Increased myocardial stiffness

→ Impaired relaxation (diastolic dysfunction)

Mechanism Link:

Neurohormonal activation (e.g., RAAS)

→ TGF-β signaling

→ Fibrosis progression

INTEGRATION – From Ca2+ Signaling to Remodeling

Chronic Ca2+ dysregulation

→ CaMKII activation (ion channel modification + arrhythmia)

→ Calcineurin activation (NFAT transcription)

→ Fetal gene reprogramming

→ Cardiomyocyte enlargement

→ Fibrosis development

→ Structural remodeling

→ Heart failure progression

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare physiological vs pathological hypertrophy.
2
Compare CaMKII vs Calcineurin mechanisms.
3
Explain how chronic Ca2+ elevation leads to gene reprogramming.
4
Explain how fibrosis contributes to heart failure.

06Day 3 缺血与再灌注

Day 3 – Ischemia & Reperfusion (Exam-Oriented Core Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based explain and compare questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Ischemia/Reperfusion module.

PART I – Ischemia: Reversible Phase

Ischemia (缺血) = Reduced blood flow → Reduced oxygen delivery

Immediate Consequence:

↓ Oxygen

→ ↓ Oxidative phosphorylation

→ ↓ ATP production

ATP depletion (ATP耗竭) leads to:

1
Ion pump failure (离子泵失效)

↓ Na+/K+ ATPase activity

→ Na+ accumulation

→ Cellular swelling (细胞肿胀)

2
Ca2+ handling impairment

→ SERCA dysfunction

→ Cytosolic Ca2+ ↑

3
Anaerobic glycolysis

→ Lactate accumulation

→ Acidosis (酸中毒)

Reversible phase logic:

ATP ↓ → Pump failure → Ionic imbalance → Cellular swelling

If blood flow restored early → Recovery possible.

PART II – Transition to Irreversible Injury

Prolonged ischemia

→ Severe ATP depletion

→ Persistent Ca2+ overload

→ Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ Membrane damage

Critical threshold:

When mitochondrial permeability transition pore (mPTP) opens

→ Loss of membrane potential

→ No ATP recovery

→ Cell death pathway triggered

PART III – Reperfusion Injury

Reperfusion (再灌注) restores oxygen but causes additional damage.

Mechanisms:

1
ROS generation (活性氧爆发)

Reoxygenation

→ Electron leak from mitochondria

→ Reactive oxygen species ↑

→ Lipid peroxidation

→ Membrane damage

2
Ca2+ overload worsens

→ SR dysfunction

→ Hypercontracture

3
Inflammation activation

→ Endothelial activation

→ Neutrophil recruitment

→ Further tissue damage

Key concept:

Reperfusion = Double-edged sword (双刃剑)

Restores oxygen but amplifies oxidative damage.

PART IV – Necrosis Mechanism

Persistent ATP depletion + Ca2+ overload + ROS

→ Membrane rupture

→ Enzyme leakage

→ Inflammatory response

→ Necrosis (坏死)

Sequence Summary:

Ischemia

→ ATP depletion

→ Ion pump failure

→ Ca2+ overload

→ Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ ROS (especially during reperfusion)

→ Irreversible membrane damage

→ Necrosis

COMPARE – Reversible vs Irreversible Injury

Reversible Injury:

• ATP reduced but not zero

• Membrane intact

• Mitochondria recoverable

• No massive Ca2+ overload

Irreversible Injury:

• Severe ATP depletion

• mPTP opening

• Membrane rupture

• Persistent Ca2+ overload

• Necrosis

INTEGRATION – Why Ischemia Leads to Heart Failure

Ischemia

→ ATP depletion

→ Contractile dysfunction

→ Ca2+ dysregulation

→ Mitochondrial damage

→ Cell death (necrosis)

→ Fibrosis replacement

→ Reduced functional myocardium

→ Heart failure progression

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Explain the full sequence from ischemia to necrosis.
2
Compare reversible vs irreversible injury.
3
Explain why reperfusion increases ROS production.
4
Explain how Ca2+ overload contributes to cell death.

07Day 4 线粒体与能量衰竭

Day 4 – Mitochondria & Energy Failure (Exam-Oriented Core Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based explanation and comparison questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Mitochondria & Energy module.

PART I – ATP Production in Cardiomyocytes

Main ATP Producers (ATP 主要来源):

1
Oxidative phosphorylation (氧化磷酸化) – mitochondria

Fatty acids (脂肪酸) – major source

Glucose oxidation (葡萄糖氧化)

TCA cycle → Electron transport chain → ATP synthesis

2
Minor contribution:

Glycolysis (糖酵解)

Key Concept:

Heart is highly oxidative organ → depends on mitochondrial ATP.

PART II – ATP Transport Systems

Creatine Kinase (CK) Shuttle (肌酸激酶穿梭):

Mitochondrial ATP

→ Converts creatine to phosphocreatine (PCr)

→ PCr diffuses to cytosol

→ ATP regenerated locally

Purpose:

Efficient spatial energy transfer.

DANC (Direct Adenine Nucleotide Channeling):

ATP diffuses directly from mitochondria to ATPases.

COMPARE:

CK Shuttle:

• Organized buffering system

• Energy reservoir (PCr)

DANC:

• Direct diffusion

• Less buffering capacity

PART III – PGC-1α and Mitochondrial Biogenesis

PGC-1α (Peroxisome proliferator-activated receptor gamma coactivator-1 alpha)

Function:

Master regulator of mitochondrial biogenesis (线粒体生成调控因子)

Mechanism:

Stress signals

→ PGC-1α activation

→ Transcription of mitochondrial genes

→ Increased oxidative capacity

In Heart Failure:

PGC-1α ↓

→ Reduced mitochondrial number

→ Reduced ATP production

→ Energy deficit

PART IV – Mitophagy

Mitophagy (线粒体自噬) = selective removal of damaged mitochondria

Key Pathway:

PINK1 / Parkin pathway

Mechanism:

Damaged mitochondria

→ PINK1 accumulation

→ Parkin recruitment

→ Ubiquitination

→ Autophagic degradation

Purpose:

Prevents ROS accumulation and mitochondrial dysfunction.

In HF:

Impaired mitophagy

→ Accumulation of dysfunctional mitochondria

→ ROS ↑

→ Energy failure

PART V – Energy Failure in Heart Failure

Chronic stress

→ Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ Reduced oxidative phosphorylation

→ ATP ↓

Consequences:

ATP depletion

→ Impaired SERCA function

→ Ca2+ dysregulation

→ Contractile dysfunction

ROS ↑

→ Membrane damage

→ Further mitochondrial impairment

Energy deficit + Ca2+ dysregulation

→ Remodeling progression

→ Heart failure

INTEGRATION – Energy-Ca2+ Feedback Loop

Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ ATP ↓

→ SERCA dysfunction

→ Ca2+ overload

→ CaMKII activation

→ Remodeling

Ca2+ overload

→ Mitochondrial stress

→ ROS ↑

→ Further ATP decline

Vicious cycle:

Energy failure ↔ Ca2+ dysregulation

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare CK shuttle vs DANC.
2
Explain how PGC-1α regulates mitochondrial biogenesis.
3
Explain how impaired mitophagy contributes to HF.
4
Explain the energy-Ca2+ vicious cycle in heart failure.

08Day 5 内皮与血管激活

Day 5 – Endothelium & Vascular Activation (Exam-Oriented Core Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based explanation and comparison questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Endothelium & Vascular Activation module.

PART I – Resting vs Activated Endothelium

Resting Endothelium (静息内皮):

• Anti-inflammatory

• Anti-thrombotic

• Low permeability

• Maintains vascular homeostasis

Activated Endothelium (激活内皮):

• Pro-inflammatory

• Pro-thrombotic

• Increased permeability

• Promotes leukocyte recruitment

Core Concept:

Endothelial activation shifts from barrier-protective to inflammation-promoting state.

PART II – Three Core Features of Endothelial Activation

1
Pro-inflammatory (促炎)

↑ Adhesion molecules

↑ Cytokine signaling

2
Pro-thrombotic (促凝)

vWF release

Platelet adhesion

3
Increased permeability (通透性增加)

Junction disruption

Plasma protein leakage

PART III – Adhesion Cascade (白细胞黏附级联反应)

Step 1 – Rolling (滚动)

Cytokines

→ P-selectin / E-selectin ↑

→ Weak leukocyte tethering

Step 2 – Activation (激活)

Chemokines bind leukocyte receptors

→ Integrin activation

Step 3 – Firm Adhesion (牢固黏附)

ICAM / VCAM ↑

→ Integrin binding

→ Stable adhesion

Step 4 – Diapedesis (穿内皮迁移)

Leukocyte transmigration into tissue

Sequence Summary:

Cytokine → Selectins → Integrins → Migration

PART IV – Weibel-Palade Bodies (WPB)

Weibel-Palade Bodies (内皮储存颗粒) contain:

• vWF (血管性血友病因子)

• P-selectin

• IL-8

• Ang2

Function:

Rapid release during endothelial activation

→ Immediate pro-thrombotic and pro-inflammatory response

PART V – Tie2–Angiopoietin System

Tie2 receptor (内皮受体)

Ang1 (血管生成素1):

→ Tie2 activation

→ Endothelial stability

→ Anti-inflammatory state

Ang2 (血管生成素2):

→ Stored in WPB

→ Antagonizes Tie2

→ Promotes activation

→ Increases permeability

Compare:

Ang1 = Stabilizing signal

Ang2 = Destabilizing / inflammatory signal

INTEGRATION – From Cytokine to Vascular Inflammation

Inflammatory cytokine stimulation

→ WPB release (vWF, P-selectin, Ang2)

→ Selectin-mediated rolling

→ ICAM/VCAM-mediated adhesion

→ Leukocyte transmigration

→ Tissue inflammation

Simultaneously:

Ang2 antagonizes Tie2

→ Barrier destabilization

→ Increased permeability

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare resting vs activated endothelium.
2
Describe the full adhesion cascade sequence.
3
Compare Ang1 vs Ang2 in Tie2 signaling.
4
Explain how endothelial activation promotes thrombosis.

09Day 6 黏膜免疫与肠道

Day 6 – Mucosal Immunology & Gut (Exam-Oriented Core Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based explanation and comparison questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Mucosal & Gut module.

PART I – Type I vs Type II Mucosa

Type I Mucosa (Type I 黏膜):

• Simple columnar epithelium (单层柱状上皮)

• Examples
Gut, respiratory tract

• Dominant antibody: IgA

• High tolerance to commensals

Type II Mucosa (Type II 黏膜):

• Stratified squamous epithelium (复层鳞状上皮)

• Examples
Oral cavity, skin-like surfaces

• Dominant antibody: IgG

• More inflammatory protection

COMPARE:

Type I = Tolerance-dominant + IgA

Type II = Inflammation-dominant + IgG

PART II – IgA Mechanisms

IgA Production Pathways:

1
T-cell dependent (依赖T细胞):

→ Germinal center reaction

→ High-affinity IgA

→ Memory B cells

2
T-cell independent (非依赖T细胞):

→ Rapid IgA production

→ Lower affinity

→ No strong memory

IgA Functions:

• Neutralization (中和)

• Immune exclusion (免疫排斥)

• Agglutination (凝集)

• Enchained growth inhibition (链式生长抑制)

IgA1 vs IgA2:

IgA1 – systemic dominant

IgA2 – mucosal dominant (protease-resistant)

PART III – Th17 vs Treg Balance

Homeostasis (稳态):

Commensal bacteria

→ Tolerogenic dendritic cells

→ IL-10 production

→ Treg ↑

→ Controlled inflammation

Infection:

Pathogen detection

→ Activated dendritic cells

→ IL-6 / IL-23 signaling

→ Th17 differentiation

→ Neutrophil recruitment

→ Inflammation ↑

COMPARE:

Treg = Anti-inflammatory, tolerance maintenance

Th17 = Pro-inflammatory, barrier defense

PART IV – Dysbiosis (Microbiota Imbalance)

Barrier disruption

→ Increased antigen exposure

→ Th17 expansion

→ Chronic inflammation

→ Tissue damage

Mechanism Summary:

Dysbiosis

→ Loss of tolerance

→ Excess Th17 response

→ Inflammatory bowel disease (IBD)-like pathology

PART V – JAK/STAT Signaling Pathway

Cytokine binding to receptor

→ JAK activation

→ STAT phosphorylation

→ STAT nuclear translocation

→ Gene transcription

Clinical Concept:

JAK inhibitors

→ Block multiple cytokine signals

→ Reduce inflammation in autoimmune diseases

INTEGRATION – From Microbiota to Inflammation

Balanced microbiota

→ Treg dominance

→ Barrier integrity maintained

Dysbiosis

→ DC activation

→ Th17 expansion

→ Chronic inflammation

→ Barrier damage

Vicious cycle:

Barrier damage ↔ Microbial imbalance

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare Type I vs Type II mucosa.
2
Compare T-cell dependent vs independent IgA production.
3
Compare Th17 vs Treg mechanisms.
4
Explain how dysbiosis leads to chronic inflammation.
5
Explain how JAK inhibitors reduce inflammation.

10Day 7 心血管药理

Day 7 – Cardiovascular Pharmacology (Exam-Oriented Core Version)

This document follows the structural logic of the top student's framework. It is optimized for mechanism-based explanation (Explain why) and comparison questions. Mastering this document is sufficient for the Pharmacology module.

PART I – α1 vs α2 Adrenergic Receptors

α1 Receptor (Gq-coupled):

Location
Vascular smooth muscle

Mechanism:

α1 activation

→ Gq

→ PLC activation

→ IP3 ↑

→ Ca2+ release

→ MLCK activation

→ Vasoconstriction

→ Blood pressure ↑

α2 Receptor (Gi-coupled):

Location
Presynaptic nerve terminals

Mechanism:

α2 activation

→ Gi

→cAMP ↓

→Norepinephrine （NE） release ↓

→sympathetic tone ↓

→ Blood pressure ↓

COMPARE:

α1 = Direct vasoconstriction (peripheral effect)

α2 = presynaptic inhibition of NE release

PART II – β1 vs β2 Adrenergic Receptors

β1 Receptor (Gs-coupled):

Location
Heart

Mechanism:

β1 activation

→ Gs

→ ↑ cAMP

→ PKA activation

→ L-type Ca2+ channel phosphorylation

→ Ca2+ influx ↑

→ Contractility ↑

→ Heart rate ↑

β2 Receptor (Gs-coupled):

Location
Vascular & bronchial smooth muscle

Mechanism:

β2 activation

→ Gs

→ ↑ cAMP

→ PKA activation

→ MLCK inhibition

→ Smooth muscle relaxation

→ Vasodilation / Bronchodilation

COMPARE:

β1 = Increases cardiac output

β2 = Causes smooth muscle relaxation

PART III – ACEI vs ARB

ACE inhibitors（ACEI）= “减少 Ang II + 增加 Bradykinin”

ACE inhibition

→ Ang II ↓

→ vasoconstriction ↓

→ aldosterone ↓

→ Na⁺/water retention ↓

→ BP ↓

Also
bradykinin breakdown ↓

→ bradykinin ↑

→ vasodilation ↑ (＋可能 cough/angioedema)

一句话记
ACEI = Ang II↓ + bradykinin↑

ARB（AT1 blockers）= “阻断 Ang II 作用”

Block AT1 receptor

→ Ang II signaling blocked

→ vasoconstriction ↓

→ aldosterone ↓

→ Na⁺/water retention ↓

→ BP ↓

bradykinin not increased

→ less cough risk

一句话记
ARB = block AT1, no bradykinin effect

COMPARE:

ACE inhibitors reduce angiotensin II production and also increase bradykinin by preventing its breakdown, enhancing vasodilation (but may cause cough).

ARBs block the AT1 receptor to prevent angiotensin II signaling and do not increase bradykinin, so they have similar hemodynamic benefits with less bradykinin-related side effects.

PART IV – Calcium Channel Blockers (CCB)

Target
L-type Ca2+ channels

Mechanism:

Block Ca2+ entry

→ ↓ Intracellular Ca2+

→ ↓ MLCK activation (in vessels)

→ Vasodilation

In heart:

↓ Ca2+ influx

→ ↓ Contractility

→ ↓ Heart rate (non-DHP type)

Types:

DHP (e.g., amlodipine):

• Mainly vascular effect

• Strong vasodilation

Non-DHP (verapamil, diltiazem):

• Cardiac + vascular effects

• Reduce heart rate & contractility

INTEGRATION – Explain Why Questions

问Why do β-blockers reduce mortality in heart failure?
答

→ ↓ Chronic β1 stimulation

→ ↓ Ca2+ overload

→ ↓ CaMKII activation

→ ↓ Remodeling progression

问Why do ACEI improve heart failure outcomes?
答

→ ↓ Afterload

→ ↓ RAAS activation

→ ↓ Fibrosis

→ ↓ Remodeling

问Why do CCB reduce blood pressure?
答

→ ↓ Ca2+ in smooth muscle

→ ↓ Vasoconstriction

→ ↓ Peripheral resistance

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Compare α1 vs α2 signaling pathways.
2
Compare β1 vs β2 physiological effects.
3
Explain why ACEI reduce remodeling in heart failure.
4
Compare ACEI vs ARB mechanisms.
5
Explain how CCB reduce blood pressure.

11Day 8 从 Ca²⁺ 失调到心衰

Day 8 – Integrated Mechanism: From Ca2+ Dysregulation to Heart Failure

This document integrates Ca2+ signaling, ischemia, mitochondrial energy failure, and remodeling into one continuous heart failure (HF) progression chain. Mastering this structure allows you to answer high-level mechanism and sequence questions.

PART I – Starting Point: Ischemia

Ischemia (reduced blood flow)

→ O₂ ↓

→ OxPhos ↓

→ ATP ↓

→ Na⁺/K⁺-ATPase ↓ → intracellular Na⁺ ↑

→ NCX Ca²⁺ extrusion ↓ / reverse → Ca²⁺ overload ↑

→ SERCA ↓ → SR Ca²⁺ reuptake ↓ → Ca²⁺ overload worsens

PART II – Ca2+ Dysregulation

A) Contractility ↓（收缩力下降链）

Ca²⁺ overload / Ca²⁺ handling impaired

→ SERCA activity ↓

→ SR Ca²⁺ reuptake ↓

→ SR Ca²⁺ content ↓ (SR depletion)

→ next beat systolic Ca²⁺ release ↓

→ Troponin activation ↓

→ Contractility ↓

一句话记
SERCA↓ → SR钙库空 → 收缩钙峰低 → 收缩弱

B) Arrhythmia ↑（心律失常链，必须补桥）

RyR leak ↑

→ diastolic intracellular Ca²⁺ ↑

→ NCX overactivation (3Na⁺ in / 1Ca²⁺ out)

→ inward depolarizing current

→ DAD (delayed afterdepolarization)

→ Arrhythmia risk ↑

一句话记
RyR漏 → 舒张钙高 → NCX去极化 → DAD → 心律失常

PART III – Mitochondrial Energy Failure

Ca2+ overload + ROS

→ Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ Reduced ATP production

→ Impaired SERCA activity

→ Further Ca2+ dysregulation

PGC-1α ↓

→ Reduced mitochondrial biogenesis

→ Energy deficit persists

Impaired mitophagy

→ Damaged mitochondria accumulate

→ ROS ↑

PART IV – Signaling Activation and Gene Reprogramming

Chronic Ca2+ elevation

→ CaMKII activation

→ Ion channel modification + arrhythmia

Sustained Ca2+ elevation

→ Calcineurin activation

→ NFAT nuclear translocation

→ Fetal gene expression

→ Pathological hypertrophy

PART V – Structural Remodeling

Chronic stress signaling

→ Cardiomyocyte enlargement

→ Fibroblast activation

→ Collagen deposition

→ Fibrosis

→ Increased stiffness

→ Reduced functional myocardium

FULL HEART FAILURE PROGRESSION CHAIN

Ischemia

→ ATP depletion

→ Ca2+ overload

→ Mitochondrial dysfunction

→ Energy failure

→ CaMKII & Calcineurin activation

→ Gene reprogramming

→ Hypertrophy

→ Fibrosis

→ Contractile dysfunction

→ Heart Failure

High-Yield Exam Explanations

Explain how ischemia leads to heart failure:

Ischemia → ATP depletion → Ca2+ dysregulation → mitochondrial damage →

pathological signaling → remodeling → loss of contractile function → HF.

Explain the vicious cycle:

Energy failure ↔ Ca2+ overload ↔ mitochondrial stress ↔ ROS production → progressive HF.

Self-Test (Must Answer Without Notes)

1
Describe the full sequence from ischemia to heart failure.
2
Explain how energy failure worsens Ca2+ dysregulation.
3
Explain how Ca2+ signaling leads to structural remodeling.
4
Describe the vicious cycle between mitochondria and Ca2+.

12Study Guide（英文）

BASIC IMMUNOLOGY

问What does the immune system do?
答

Normal (physiologic) roles:

Immunity - protection against infection

Defense against tumors

Sustain organ function

Roles in disease:

Cause immunological diseases (allergies, autoimmune diseases)

Suspected role in non-immune diseases (atherosclerosis, Alzheimer’s disease, type 2 diabetes)

Types of immune cells

Origin: Stem cell differentiation from bone marrow (Hematopoietic stem cell - HSC)

Effector cells: leukocytes (WBCs) that eliminate microbes (which is the effect of the immune response); may be lymphocytes, but mainly other leukocytes

Lymphocytes: cells that play a role in adaptive immunity, recognize antigens and differentiate into cells that perform defense functions (T cells & B cells), or innate immunity (NK cells)

Antigen-presenting cells: cells that capture antigens and display them to lymphocytes (dendritic cells & macrophages)

Types of immune response

Innate immunity: always present, ready to attach, many pathogenic microbes have evolved to resist innate immunity

Adaptive immunity: stimulated by exposure to microbe, more potent, takes time to have an effect

Types of Lymphocytes

Innate lymphocytes:

Innate lymphoid cells (ILCs): no TCR

ILC1 - NK cells: microbes, viruses

ILC2: allergy, parasites

ILC3: gut barrier, bacteria

→ Early immunity before adaptive T-cells arrive

Innate-like lymphocytes (ILLs): invariant TCR

→ Have receptors, but behave quickly like innate immune cells, with limited variability of TCR

Adaptive lymphocytes:

T cells

B cells

→ Has memory, and is specific and more potent to antigens

Main types of Lymphocytes and their functions

B lymphocytes:

Produce antibodies that neutralize microbes

Phagocytosis

Complement system activation (plasma proteins → kill microbes, enhance inflammation, opsonize pathogens)

Helper T lymphocytes (CD4+) - Master controller of immune responses:

Activate macrophages

Induce inflammation

Activate T cells & B cells

Cytotoxic T cells (CD8+):

Kill infected cells

Kill tumor cells

Natural killer cells (NK cells):

Kill virus-infected cells

Kill tumor cells

Regulatory T cells (Tregs):

Suppression of other T cells

Prevent excessive inflammation & auto-immunity

Types of Adaptive Immunity:

Humoral Immunity:

B cells produce antibodies

Antibodies circulate in the serum

→ Block infections, eliminate extracellular microbes

Cell-mediated Immunity:

Helper T cells (CD4+) activate macrophages

→ Phagocytosis of microbes

Cytotoxic T cells (CD8+) kill infected cells, tumor cells

→ Eliminate reservoirs of infection

Stages in the life history of lymphocytes:

Naive cell → Antigen recognition

Proliferate & differentiate into activated or effector lymphocytes

Some differentiate into memory lymphocytes

General Principles of Immune Responses

Innate Immunity

Pathogen → Sensor / Effector → Response

Pathogen → Sensor → 1st order cytokine → Effector → Response

Innate OR Adaptive Immunity (Depending on Lymphocyte type)

Pathogen → Sensor → 1st order cytokine → Lymphocyte → 2nd order cytokine → Effector → Response

1st order and 2nd order cytokines

1st order cytokines: Produced immediately by innate immune cells after sensing pathogens

Roles of 1st order cytokines

Initiate & shape immune responses

Activate lymphocytes

Drive T cell differentiation

2nd order cytokines: Produced by lymphocytes after they are activated by 1st order cytokines

Roles of 2nd order cytokines

Execute effector functions

Drive later stages of immune response

Recruit immune cells

Promote class-switching, inflammation, tolerance

Inflammation - Start of Immune Response

When there is a wound, pathogens will enter the tissue via that wound

Resident macrophages will perform pathogen phagocytosis. At the same time, they will produce cytokines to recruit other immune cells

Mast cells present at the site will also secrete histamines, which induce vasodilation and increase vessel leakiness

Because of this, neutrophils can exit the blood stream and enter the tissue via extravasation. They are the main first-line responders and will kill the pathogens

(Extravasation steps: rolling, adhesion, diapedesis, and migration)

After that, more macrophages will arrive, and they will clean up debris, remove dead cells, and promote tissue repair

Weibel-Palade Bodies (WPBs):

Location: Vascular Endothelial Cells

Storage granules for:

von Willebrand factor (vWF): a blood glycoprotein that promotes primary hemostasis (specifically platelet adhesion)

P-selectin: mediate leukocyte rolling in early inflammation

IL-8 (in previously-stimulated cells): recruit neutrophils to inflammation site

→ Contribute to the rolling, activation, and diapedesis of leukocytes in blood vessels

Antigen Presenting Cells (APCs):

Dendritic cells

Macrophages

B cells

T cell priming: Dendritic cells carry antigen into the lymph nodes for T cell priming → Activate naive T cells in lymphoid organs

Naive T cell entry into lymph nodes

Rolling: mediated by selectins

Activation: mediated by chemokines

Adhesion: mediated by integrins

Diapedesis: mediated by chemokines

Dendritic cell maturation (before T cell activation)

Immature DCs sit in mucosal tissues and constantly sample the environment

Immature DCs encounter pathogen-associated molecular patterns (PAMPs) → Activated DCs

Express MHC II → Helper T cell (CD4+) activation

T cell activation signals

MHC II - TCR interaction → T cell activation

CD80 - CD28 interaction → T cell survival

Cytokine secretion (from APCs) → T cell differentiation

Lymphocyte circulation pattern

Blood → Lymph node (via HEVs - High Endothelial Venules) → Efferent lymphatic → Thoracic duct → Blood

Blood → Body tissues → Lymph node → Efferent lymphatic → Thoracic duct → Blood → Tissues

Blood → Spleen → Blood

→ Constantly scan the body for any infections

T-dependent & T-independent antibody responses

T-dependent antibody responses:

Protein antigens can be presented on MHC II

→ Activate Follicular Helper T cells (Tfh)

→ Promote:

Class switching (IgM → IgG, IgA, IgE)

Affinity maturation (antibodies become stronger)

Memory B cell formation (long-term immunity)

→ Strong, long-lasting, high-quality antibody response,

long-lived plasma cells

T-independent antibody responses:

Polysaccharide and lipid antigens cannot be presented on MHC II → Direct B cell activation

→ Effects:

IgM production only

Low affinity antibodies

Little memory

→ Fast but weak & short-lived responses (short-lived

plasma cells)

Steps in T-dependent B cell activation

DC activates CD4⁺ T cell (T-cell zone)

B cell binds same antigen (B-cell follicle)

T and B meet at T–B border → B cell activation

B cell becomes either:

Short-lived plasma cells (extrafollicular): Secrete mainly IgMs & some IgGs (low affinity)

Enters germinal center → class switching, affinity maturation, memory (high affinity) → Long-lived plasma cells

Memory B cells

B cell activation signals:
